-value appears because of the decomposition of Proposition
3.7
.

4.
A
p
-adic tower of level subgroups and its Iwasawa theory

In what follows, we use the method described succinctly in Loeffler’s survey
[
32
]
and
[
31
]
to show that our cycles vary in
p
-adic families, in a suitable cohomological sense. The results in
loc. cit.
do not apply directly to our setting exactly because of Milne’s axiom SV5 forcing the centre to contain no
{\mathbb{R}}
-split torus which is not already
{\mathbb{Q}}
-split. This excludes Hilbert modular varieties most notably. We provide the necessary details to show that this hypothesis can be removed in our case. This relies on the fact that the open compact level subgroups in our
p
-adic tower all share the same intersection with the center of
G
.

Recall that
L
denotes a number field over which
H
and
G
split. Throughout the rest of the manuscript, we fix a prime
\mathfrak{m}
of
L
above
p
and denote by
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}
the localization of the ring of integers
\mathcal{O}:=\mathcal{O}_{L}
at
\mathfrak{m}
.

4.1.
A spherical variety

Let
B_{G}=T_{G}\cdot N_{G}
denote the upper-triangular Borel subgroup of
G
with Levi subgroup the maximal diagonal torus
T_{G}
and unipotent radical
N_{G}
. We also let
\overline{B}_{G}=T_{G}\cdot\overline{N}_{G}
be the opposite parabolic. Similarly, denote by
B_{H}=T_{H}\cdot N_{H}=B_{G}\cap H
the upper-triangular Borel subgroup of
H
.
Consider the flag variety
\mathcal{F}:=G/\overline{B}_{G}
over
{\rm Spec}\,{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. It has a natural left action by
G
and, thus, a left action by
H=G_{F}
via the embedding
\iota
. Recall that
H({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}|p}\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}})
over primes
\mathfrak{p}
in
F
with
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}}:={\mathcal{O}}_{F,\mathfrak{p}}
, and
G({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\prod_{\mathfrak{P}|p}\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}})
over primes
\mathfrak{P}
in
E
with
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}}:={\mathcal{O}}_{E,\mathfrak{P}}
. Via these identifications
\iota:H({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\to G({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
can be written as
(\iota_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{p}}
where each embedding

\iota_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}})\hookrightarrow\prod_{\mathfrak{P}|\mathfrak{p}}\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}})

is the natural map induced by the inclusion of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
in
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}}
. If
\mathfrak{p}
splits in
E
, then
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{P}\overline{\mathfrak{P}}
(thus
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}}={\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}}={\mathcal{O}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{P}}}
) and
\iota_{\mathfrak{p}}(g)=(g,g)
. If
\mathfrak{p}
is inert in
E
, there is only one prime
\mathfrak{P}
above it and
\iota_{\mathfrak{p}}
identifies
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}})
with the
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}}
-valued matrices fixed by
{\rm Gal}(E_{\mathfrak{P}}/F_{\mathfrak{p}})
.

Let
\Upsilon_{F,p}
denote the set of primes of
F
above
p
. This can be divided into two subsets
\Upsilon_{F,p}=\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}\cup\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}
, where
\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}
(resp.
\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}
) is the set of primes above
p
which are split (resp. inert) in
E
. Then we may write

G({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}}\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}})^{2}\times\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}}\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}}).

Definition 4.1
.

Let
u\in G({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
denote the element
(u_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}
given by

u_{\mathfrak{p}}=\begin{cases}\left({{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}1&1\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}},{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\right)&\text{ if }\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p},\\
{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}&\text{ if }\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p},\end{cases}

with
\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}}\setminus{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
such that
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}}={\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}
, and
\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

Finally, we let
L_{H}
denote the torus of
H({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
given by

L_{H}=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}}T_{(1,0)}\times\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}}T_{\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}},

where
T_{(1,0)}
denotes the conjugate
6
6

6

Note that
T_{(1,0)}=yT_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}y^{-1}
, with
y={{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
1&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}
.
of the maximal split torus of of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}})
given by

\{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&\\
a-d&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}})\},

while
T_{\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}}
denotes the maximal non-split torus of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}})
given by

\{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&c\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}\\
c&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,:\,(a,c)\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}\text{ such that }a+c\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\times}\}.

Lemma 4.2
.

(1)

The
H
-orbit of
u
in
\mathcal{F}/{\rm Spec}\,{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
is Zariski open,

(2)

{\rm Stab}_{H}(u\overline{B}_{G})({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\iota(L_{H}).

Proof.

We start with calculating the stabilizer of
u\overline{B}_{G}
, which is

{\rm Stab}_{H}(u\overline{B}_{G})=\iota(H)\cap u\overline{B}_{G}u^{-1}.

Let
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}
, then
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}})\ni g={{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}
fixes
[u_{\mathfrak{p}}]
if
g
and
{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}1&-1\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\cdot g\cdot{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}1&1\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}
are lower-triangular. As

u_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\cdot g\cdot u_{\mathfrak{p}}=\left({{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a-c&a+b-c-d\\
c&d+c\end{smallmatrix}\big)}},{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\right),

we deduce that
g={{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&\\
a-d&d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}
. Therefore any element in
u_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}})u_{\mathfrak{p}}\cap\overline{B}_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}})^{2}
is of the form
\left({{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}d&\\
a-d&a\end{smallmatrix}\big)}},{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&\\
a-d&d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\right)
. In other words,
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap u_{\mathfrak{p}}\overline{B}_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}})^{2}u_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}=\iota_{\mathfrak{p}}(T_{(1,0)})
.

Let
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}
. Keeping the notation as above,

u_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\cdot{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\cdot u_{\mathfrak{p}}={{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a-\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}c&\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}(a-d)+b-\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}c\\
c&d+\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}c\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\in\overline{B}_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}})

implies that
b=\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}c
and that
d=a
. Thus, in this case
g\in T_{\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}}
. Hence
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}})\cap u_{\mathfrak{p}}\overline{B}_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}})u_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}=\iota_{\mathfrak{p}}(T_{\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}})
.

Summing up, we deduce that
{\rm Stab}_{H}(u\overline{B}_{G})({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\iota(L_{H})
. Finally, notice that the
H
-orbit of
u
is open for the Zariski topology as its dimension equals the dimension of
\mathcal{F}
.
∎

Definition 4.3
.

We let
L_{G}
be the subtorus of
T_{G}
given by
L_{G}=\{{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\alpha&\\
&\tau(\alpha)\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\,:\,\alpha\in{\rm Res}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}/{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathbf{G}_{\rm m}\}
and denote
Q_{G}:=L_{G}\cdot N_{G}
,
\overline{Q}_{G}:=L_{G}\cdot\overline{N}_{G}
.

Corollary 4.4
.

We have an inclusion
u^{-1}\iota(L_{H})u\subset\overline{Q}_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\subset\overline{B}_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
.

Proof.

This follows directly from the proof of the Lemma above. For instance, at an inert prime
\mathfrak{p}
, if
g=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&c\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}\\
c&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\iota_{\mathfrak{p}}(T_{\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}})
, then

u_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}gu_{\mathfrak{p}}={{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a-\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}c&\\
c&a+\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}c\end{smallmatrix}\big)}},

which is lower-triangular, with diagonal elements conjugate to each other as desired.
∎

In the course of the proof of Theorem
5.4
, we will need an explicit description of the action of
u^{-1}
on the invariant vector
\Delta^{\underline{k}}\in V^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}
introduced in Formula (
3
). We record it here. The element
u
or its inverse act on the
G(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})
-lattice
V^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}
via the embedding

j:G({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{E}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\hookrightarrow G(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})=\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{E}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})\simeq\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})^{2d},

which we now recall. Write
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}_{E}=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}}\mathcal{O}^{2}_{\mathfrak{p}}\times\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}}
as above, so that

G({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}}\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}})^{2}\times\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}}\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}}).

At each
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}
, then
j
is induced by the natural embedding of
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{d_{\mathfrak{p}}}
. At each
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}
,
j
is also induced by the embedding
\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{m}})
together with the isomorphism
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{m}})\simeq({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{m}})^{2}
given by sending
\alpha\otimes\beta\mapsto(\beta i_{\mathfrak{P}}(\alpha),\beta i_{\mathfrak{P}}(\bar{\alpha}))
, with
i_{\mathfrak{P}}:{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{m}}
, and the isomorphism
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{d_{\mathfrak{p}}}
. Note that the sum of the
d_{\mathfrak{p}}
’s is
d
, and when
p
is totally split in
F
then
d_{\mathfrak{p}}=1
for every
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}
.

Definition 4.5
.

The fixed isomorphism
i_{p}:{\mathbb{C}}\simeq\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
yields a partition of
\Sigma_{F}=\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}
, where, for every prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}
, we let
\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}
be the subset of
\Sigma_{F}
of (
p
-adic) places
\sigma
sending
\mathfrak{p}
to the maximal ideal of the valuation ring of
F_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Similarly, this induces a partition
\Phi=\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

Note that, for each
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}
,
\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}
consist of
d_{\mathfrak{p}}
elements.

At each
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}
,
u^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}
acts on the components in
\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}
of
\Delta^{\underline{k}}
via the action of
\left({{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}1&-1\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}},{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\right)
, which sends
\Delta_{\sigma}=(e_{1,\sigma}e_{2,\sigma^{\prime}}-e_{2,\sigma}e_{1,\sigma^{\prime}})
to
(e_{1,\sigma}e_{1,\sigma^{\prime}})+\Delta_{\sigma}
and so, taking the
k_{\sigma}
tensor product, it sends
\Delta_{\sigma}^{k_{\sigma}}=(e_{1,\sigma}e_{2,\sigma^{\prime}}-e_{2,\sigma}e_{1,\sigma^{\prime}})^{\otimes{k_{\sigma}}}
to
(e_{1,\sigma}e_{1,\sigma^{\prime}})^{\otimes{k_{\sigma}}}+R_{\sigma}^{k_{\sigma}}
, where
R_{\sigma}^{k_{\sigma}}
has zero projection to the highest weight eigenspace.

At each
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}
, recall we have fixed
\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}}
such that
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{P}}={\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}}\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Then
u^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}
acts on the components in
\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}
of
\Delta^{\underline{k}}
via the action of
\left({{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}1&-i_{\mathfrak{P}}(\delta_{\mathfrak{p}})\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}},{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}1&i_{\mathfrak{P}}(\delta_{\mathfrak{p}})\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\right)
, which sends
\Delta_{\sigma}
to
(2i_{\mathfrak{P}}(\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}))(e_{1,\sigma}e_{1,\sigma^{\prime}})+\Delta_{\sigma}
. Summing up, we have shown the following :

Lemma 4.6
.

We have that

u^{-1}\cdot\Delta^{\underline{k}}=a_{\underline{k}}\cdot v_{\underline{k}}^{\rm hw}+R^{\underline{k}},

where
v_{\underline{k}}^{\rm hw}
is the highest weight vector in
V^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}
,
a_{\underline{k}}=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}}(2i_{\mathfrak{P}}(\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}))^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}}k_{\sigma}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\times}
, and
R^{\underline{k}}\in V^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}
has no
v_{\underline{k}}^{\rm hw}
-component.

4.2.
Level subgroups and Hecke operators

Let
\eta
denote the diagonal matrix
{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}p&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\in G({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
and let

\Gamma_{0}(p^{r}):=\{g\in G({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\,:\,g\,[\text{mod }p^{r}]\in B_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}/p^{r}{\mathbb{Z}})\}

be the Iwahori subgroup of
G({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
. Similarly,
\Gamma^{0}(p^{r})
denotes the opposite Iwahori subgroup of level
p^{r}
of
G({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, while
\Gamma^{0}_{0}(p^{r},p^{s})
denotes the intersection of
\Gamma_{0}(p^{r})
with
\Gamma^{0}(p^{s})
. Following
[
32
]
, we define the following level subgroups.

Definition 4.7
.

Fix
K^{(p)}\subseteq G({\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}^{(p)}})
a neat open compact subgroup and for
r\geq 0
let

\displaystyle K_{r}

\displaystyle:=K^{(p)}\cdot\{g\in\Gamma_{0}(p)\,:\,\eta^{-r}g\eta^{r}\in G({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}),\;g\,[\text{mod }p^{r}]\in\overline{Q}_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}/p^{r}{\mathbb{Z}})\},

\displaystyle K_{r}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=K^{(p)}\cdot\{g\in\Gamma_{0}(p)\,:\,\eta^{-r-1}g\eta^{r+1}\in G({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}),\;g\,[\text{mod }p^{r}]\in\overline{Q}_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}/p^{r}{\mathbb{Z}})\},

\displaystyle V_{r}

\displaystyle:=\eta^{-r}K_{r}\eta^{r},

\displaystyle V_{r}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\eta^{-r-1}K_{r}^{\prime}\eta^{r+1}.

Remark 4.8
.

•

Explicitly,
K_{0}=K^{(p)}\cdot\Gamma_{0}(p)
,
K_{0}^{\prime}=K^{(p)}\cdot\Gamma_{0}^{0}(p,p)
, and

K_{1}=K^{(p)}\cdot\{g\in G({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\,:\,g\,[\text{mod }p]\in L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}/p{\mathbb{Z}})\}.

•

Notice that
V_{0}=K_{0}
,
V_{0}^{\prime}=K^{(p)}\cdot\eta^{-1}\Gamma_{0}^{0}(p,p)\eta=K^{(p)}\cdot\Gamma_{0}(p^{2})
. In general,

V_{r}=K^{(p)}\cdot\{g\in\Gamma_{0}(p^{r+1})\,:\,g\,[\text{mod }p^{r}]\in Q_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}/p^{r}{\mathbb{Z}})\}.

The tower of subgroups
(V_{r})_{r}
will appear when we define our Iwasawa cohomology groups.

•

K_{r}^{\prime}
is the natural subgroup used to define the Hecke correspondence for the “
U_{p}
”-operator associated with
\eta^{-1}
.

Note that
K_{r+1}\subseteq K_{r}^{\prime}=K_{r}\cap\eta K_{r}\eta^{-1}\subseteq K_{r}
. This implies that right multiplication by
\eta
gives a morphism

[\eta]_{r+1,r}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 26.14659pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-26.14659pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 50.14659pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.14659pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 290.95424pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 290.95424pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{r}),}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

where
{\rm pr}
denote natural projections.

We note that we can refine Definition
4.7
by defining towers of level subgroups for each subset of
\Upsilon_{F,p}
, as follows.

For each
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}
, let
\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}:={\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(F_{\mathfrak{p}})
and denote in the same way the corresponding element of
H({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
with trivial component at every other place in
\Upsilon_{F,p}
. Moreover, for any
r\geq 0
set
K_{\mathfrak{p},r}
to be either

\left\{(g_{1},g_{2})\in{\mathrm{GL}}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}})^{2}\,:\,(g_{1},g_{2})\equiv\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&\\
x_{1}&d\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}d&\\
x_{2}&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\,[\text{mod }\mathfrak{p}^{r}]\right\}\cap\Gamma_{0}(\pi_{\mathfrak{p}})^{2}

if
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}
, or

\left\{g\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})\,:\,g\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\alpha&\\
x&\tau(\alpha)\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,[\text{mod }\mathfrak{P}^{r}]\right\}\cap\Gamma_{0}(\pi_{\mathfrak{p}})

if
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}
, with
\mathfrak{P}
denoting the prime of
E
above
\mathfrak{p}
. We then let
K_{\mathfrak{p},r}^{\prime}:=K_{\mathfrak{p},r}\cap\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}K_{\mathfrak{p},r}\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}
,
V_{\mathfrak{p},r}:=\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-r}K_{\mathfrak{p},r}\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{r}
, and
V_{\mathfrak{p},r}^{\prime}:=\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-r-1}K_{\mathfrak{p},r}\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{r+1}
.

Definition 4.9
.

For any multi-index
\boldsymbol{r}=(r_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{p}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{|\Upsilon_{F,p}|}
, we define

\displaystyle K_{\boldsymbol{r}}

\displaystyle:=K^{(p)}\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}K_{\mathfrak{p},r_{\mathfrak{p}}},

\displaystyle V_{\boldsymbol{r}}

\displaystyle:=K^{(p)}\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}V_{\mathfrak{p},r_{\mathfrak{p}}},

and similarly for
K_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\prime}
and
V_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\prime}
.

When
\boldsymbol{r}=(r)_{\mathfrak{p}}
, then these level groups agree with those of Definition
4.7
.

Later on, if

\mathfrak{S}
is any subset of
\Upsilon_{F,p}
, we also consider

(13)

\displaystyle K_{0}(\mathfrak{S}):=K^{(p)}\cdot(\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{S}}\Gamma^{0}_{0}(\pi_{\mathfrak{p}},\pi_{\mathfrak{p}})\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}\smallsetminus\mathfrak{S}}\Gamma_{0}(\pi_{\mathfrak{p}})).

We have

K_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\prime}=K_{0}^{\prime}\subseteq K_{0}(\mathfrak{S})\subseteq K_{\boldsymbol{0}}=K_{0},

where the symbol
\boldsymbol{0}
denotes the multi-index
(0,\dots,0)
.

We now define certain Hecke operators that play a crucial role in the manuscript.

Lemma 4.10
.

There is a well-defined action of
p^{\sum_{\sigma}{(k_{\sigma}+m)/2}}\cdot\eta^{-1}
on
V_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\underline{k},m}
which induces a morphism of local systems

\eta_{\flat}:\mathscr{V}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\underline{k},m}\to\eta^{*}\mathscr{V}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\underline{k},m}.

In particular we have a map

{\rm Tr}_{\eta}:H^{i}(Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\prime}),\mathscr{V}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\underline{k},m})\longrightarrow H^{i}(Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}}),\mathscr{V}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\underline{k},m})

defined by composing the map in cohomology induced by
\eta_{\flat}
with the trace of
{\rm pr}\circ\eta:Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\prime})\to Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}})
in Betti cohomology.

Proof.

Note that the torus
\{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}x&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\}
acts on each weight space of
{\rm Sym}^{k_{\sigma}}(V_{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{det}_{\sigma}^{\frac{m-k_{\sigma}}{2}}
by the character
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}x&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\mapsto x^{\frac{m+k_{\sigma}-2\ell_{\sigma}}{2}}
, where
\ell_{\sigma}
is an integer ranging between
0
to
k_{\sigma}
. Hence, the matrix
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-1}&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)
acts on each weight space of
{\rm Sym}^{k_{\sigma}}(V_{\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{det}_{\sigma}^{\frac{m-k_{\sigma}}{2}}
by
p^{\frac{2\ell_{\sigma}-k_{\sigma}-m}{2}}
. This implies that the action of
\eta^{-1}
on each weight space of
V^{\underline{k},m}
will be given by
p^{\sum_{\sigma}{(2\ell_{\sigma}-k_{\sigma}-m)/2}}
, for some
\underline{\ell}=(\ell_{\sigma})_{\sigma}
such that
0\leq\ell_{\sigma}\leq k_{\sigma}
. In particular,
\eta^{-1}
acts on the highest weight subspace
L_{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot v_{\underline{k},m}^{\rm hw}
by
p^{-\sum_{\sigma}{(k_{\sigma}+m)/2}}
.
As every admissible lattice is the direct sum of its intersections with the weight spaces of
V^{\underline{k},m}
, we get an action of
p^{\sum_{\sigma}{(k_{\sigma}+m)/2}}\cdot\eta^{-1}
on
V^{\underline{k},m}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}
such that it is trivial on the highest weight subspace
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot v_{\underline{k},m}^{\rm hw}
and divisible by a positive power of
p
on every other weight subspace. By letting
\eta_{\flat}:\mathscr{V}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\underline{k},m}\to\eta^{*}\mathscr{V}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\underline{k},m}
be the corresponding morphism of local systems, we get the result.
∎

Definition 4.11
.

Let the symbol
\boldsymbol{1}
denote the multi-index with all entries equal to
1
. We define

{\rm Tr}_{[\eta]_{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1},\boldsymbol{r}}}:H^{i}(Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}),\mathscr{V}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\underline{k},m})\longrightarrow H^{i}(Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}}),\mathscr{V}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\underline{k},m})

as the composition of the trace of the natural projection
Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}})\to Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\prime})
with
{\rm Tr}_{\eta}
.

Remark 4.12
.

It is clear from the proof of the Lemma above that the trace
{\rm Tr}_{\eta}
is optimally normalized so that
p^{\sum_{\sigma}{(k_{\sigma}+m)/2}}\cdot\eta^{-1}
acts as
1
on the highest weight subspace and divisible by
p
elsewhere.

Definition 4.13
.

We let
T_{\eta}
be the Hecke correspondence acting on cohomology given by
{\rm Tr}_{\eta}\circ(\pi_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{G})^{*}
, where
\pi_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{G}:Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\prime})\to Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}})
is the natural degeneracy map and
{\rm Tr}_{\eta}
is the map defined in Lemma
4.10
.

Note that
T_{\eta}
is the correspondence associated with the Hecke operator
[K_{\boldsymbol{r}}\eta^{-1}K_{\boldsymbol{r}}]
in the Hecke algebra
\mathcal{H}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}}\backslash G({\mathbb{A}}_{f})/K_{\boldsymbol{r}})_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}
of
K_{\boldsymbol{r}}
-bi-invariant smooth compactly supported
v
-valued functions on
G({\mathbb{A}}_{f})
.

Remark 4.14
.

In the same analogous way, attached to each
\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}
, we have the Hecke operator
T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}
, which we normalize so that

T_{\eta}=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}.

Concretely, we normalize
T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}
as follows.
Recall that, in Definition
4.5
, we have fixed a partition of
\Sigma_{F}=\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Then, we multiply the trace of
\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}
by
p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}(k_{\sigma}+k_{\sigma^{\prime}}+2m)/2}
, where we identify
\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}
with the subset
\Phi_{\mathfrak{p}}
of the
F
-type
\Phi
.
Note that the (normalized) trace
{\rm Tr}_{[\eta]_{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1},\boldsymbol{r}}}
is equal to the composition of the (normalized) traces

{\rm Tr}_{[\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}]_{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1},\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}}}:H^{i}(Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}),\mathscr{V}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\underline{k},m})\longrightarrow H^{i}(Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}}),\mathscr{V}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\underline{k},m}),

with
\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}
equal to
\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}
away from
\mathfrak{p}
and equal to
\boldsymbol{r}
at
\mathfrak{p}
, as
\mathfrak{p}
runs over
\Upsilon_{F,p}
.

4.3.
A few Cartesian diagrams

Let
u\in G({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
denote the element of Definition
4.1
. For any multi-index
\boldsymbol{r}=(r_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{p}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{|\Upsilon_{F,p}|}
, we have the morphism

\textstyle{u\circ\iota_{\boldsymbol{r}}:Y_{H}(uK_{\boldsymbol{r}}u^{-1}\cap H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\boldsymbol{r}}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(uK_{\boldsymbol{r}}u^{-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}}).}

Lemma 4.15
.

Suppose that there exists a neat open compact subgroup
U
of
G({\mathbb{A}}_{f})
containing
K_{\boldsymbol{r}}
and its conjugate
K_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\tau}
via the non-trivial element
\tau\in{\rm Gal}(E/F)
. Then the map
u\circ\iota_{\boldsymbol{r}}
is a closed immersion.

Proof.

Notice that it is enough to check it at the level of
{\mathbb{C}}
-points. As right multiplication by
u
is clearly a bijection, the statement follows from showing that
\iota_{\boldsymbol{r}}
is injective. To ease the notation, let
V
denote
uK_{\boldsymbol{r}}u^{-1}
. Recall that, if we denote by
Y_{H}
and
Y_{G}
the varieties at infinite level, we have using
[
11
, (1.8.2)]
and that the center acts trivially

Y_{H}({\mathbb{C}})=\tfrac{H({\mathbb{Q}})}{Z_{H}({\mathbb{Q}})}\backslash\left(X_{H}\times H({\mathbb{A}}_{f})/\overline{Z_{H}({\mathbb{Q}})}\right),

where
\overline{Z_{H}({\mathbb{Q}})}
denotes the closure of
Z_{H}({\mathbb{Q}})
in
Z_{H}({\mathbb{A}}_{f})
and where
X_{H}
is isomorphic to
d
copies of the union
\mathfrak{H}\cup\mathfrak{H}^{-}
of upper and lower half planes. Similarly,

Y_{G}({\mathbb{C}})=\tfrac{G({\mathbb{Q}})}{Z_{G}({\mathbb{Q}})}\backslash\left(X_{G}\times G({\mathbb{A}}_{f})/\overline{Z_{G}({\mathbb{Q}})}\right),

with
X_{G}
isomorphic to
2d
copies of
\mathfrak{H}\cup\mathfrak{H}^{-}
. Note that
\iota:H\to G
induces an injection
Y_{H}({\mathbb{C}})\hookrightarrow Y_{G}({\mathbb{C}})
since, in this case,

\overline{Z_{G}({\mathbb{Q}})}\cap H({\mathbb{A}}_{f})=\overline{Z_{H}({\mathbb{Q}})}.

The result thus follows from showing that
Y_{H}({\mathbb{C}})\cap Y_{H}({\mathbb{C}})v=\emptyset
for any
v\in V\smallsetminus V\cap H({\mathbb{A}}_{f})
. We show this fact as follows.
Firstly, notice that the non-trivial element
\tau\in{\rm Gal}(E/F)
induces a map
\tau:Y_{G}({\mathbb{C}})\to Y_{G}({\mathbb{C}})
, which fixes
Y_{H}({\mathbb{C}})
point-wise.
Suppose now that
y,yv\in Y_{H}({\mathbb{C}})
for
v\in V
. Then
v^{-1}v^{\tau}
fixes
y
. Since
v^{-1}v^{\tau}
belongs to
uUu^{-1}
and, by the neatness assumption, we deduce
v^{-1}v^{\tau}=1
, so
v=v^{\tau}
, which implies that
v\in H({\mathbb{A}}_{f})
, as desired.
∎

Remark 4.16
.

The hypothesis of Lemma
4.15
is far from being necessary, but as it will always be the case in our applications, we are content with it.

Observe that, for instance, the hypothesis of Lemma
4.15
is satisfied whenever
K^{(p)}
is stable under the non-trivial element
\tau\in{\rm Gal}(E/F)
. From now on, we then assume that this is the case.

Proposition 4.17
.

Assume that
K^{(p)}
is stable under
\tau\in{\rm Gal}(E/F)
and let
\boldsymbol{r}=(r_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{p}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}^{|\Upsilon_{F,p}|}
. The commutative diagram

\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm pr}}
\scriptstyle{[\eta]_{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1},\boldsymbol{r}}}
\textstyle{Y_{H}(uK_{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}u^{-1}\cap H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{H}}
\scriptstyle{u\circ\iota_{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm pr}\circ u\circ\iota_{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{G}}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\textstyle{Y_{H}(uK_{\boldsymbol{r}}u^{-1}\cap H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u\circ\iota_{\boldsymbol{r}}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{\eta}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}})}

has Cartesian bottom square. Here
\pi_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{H},\pi_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{G}
denote the natural degeneracy maps.

Proof.

To show that the diagram is Cartesian, it is enough to check it for the complex points of the varieties. Then the result follows from showing that

(1)

u\circ\iota_{\boldsymbol{r}}
and
{\rm pr}\circ u\circ\iota_{{\boldsymbol{r}}+\boldsymbol{1}}
are closed immersions,

(2)

The maps
\pi_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{H}
and
\pi_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{G}
are covering maps of the same degree.

By Lemma
4.15
,
u\circ\iota_{\boldsymbol{s}}
is a closed immersion for any
{\boldsymbol{s}}
, therefore (1) follows from showing that
{\rm pr}\circ u\circ\iota_{{\boldsymbol{r}}+\boldsymbol{1}}
is a closed immersion, i.e.

uK^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{r}}u^{-1}\cap H=uK_{{\boldsymbol{r}}+\boldsymbol{1}}u^{-1}\cap H.

This follows as in the proof of
[
32
, Lemma 4.4.1(i)]
from the calculation of Lemma
4.2
(2) applied modulo
p^{{\boldsymbol{r}}+{\boldsymbol{1}}}
. Here, for any multi-index
r
, we denote
p^{{\boldsymbol{r}}}:=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathfrak{p}^{r_{\mathfrak{p}}}
.

Notice that
K_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\prime}\cap Z_{G}({\mathbb{Q}})=K_{\boldsymbol{r}}\cap Z_{G}({\mathbb{Q}})
. Indeed, if
\gamma\in K_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\prime}\cap Z_{G}({\mathbb{Q}})
, then at
p
,
\eta^{-\boldsymbol{r}-\boldsymbol{1}}\gamma\eta^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}=\gamma\in G({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, with
\eta^{\boldsymbol{s}}:=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}}\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{s_{\mathfrak{p}}}
, and
\gamma\,[\text{mod }p^{\boldsymbol{r}}]\in\overline{Q}_{G}
. These are exactly the conditions at
p
characterising the intersection
K_{\boldsymbol{r}}\cap Z_{G}({\mathbb{Q}})
. Hence, by Lemma
4.18
, the degrees of the maps
\pi_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{H}
and
\pi_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{G}
equal to
[uK_{\boldsymbol{r}}u^{-1}\cap H:uK_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\prime}u^{-1}\cap H]
and
[K_{\boldsymbol{r}}:K_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\prime}]
respectively. We are thus left to show that

[uK_{\boldsymbol{r}}u^{-1}\cap H:uK_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\prime}u^{-1}\cap H]=[K_{\boldsymbol{r}}:K_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\prime}].

This follows as in the proof of
[
32
, Lemma 4.4.1(ii)]
from the fact that the
H
-orbit of
u\overline{B}_{G}
is open as a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-subscheme of
\mathcal{F}
(
cf
. Lemma
4.2
(1)).
∎

Proposition
4.17
does not follow directly from
[
32
]
as our Shimura data do not satisfy the Axiom SV5. However, to circumvent this obstruction it suffices to use the following:

Lemma 4.18
.

Let
K,K^{\prime}\subset G({\mathbb{A}}_{f})
be neat compact open subgroups such that
K^{\prime}\subset K
and
K\cap Z_{G}({\mathbb{Q}})=K^{\prime}\cap Z_{G}({\mathbb{Q}})
. Then, the map
{\rm pr}_{K^{\prime},K}:Y_{G}(K^{\prime})({\mathbb{C}})\twoheadrightarrow Y_{G}(K)({\mathbb{C}})
is a covering map of smooth complex manifolds of degree
[K:K^{\prime}]
.

Proof.

See for instance
[
39
, Lemma 2.7.1]
.
∎

Remark 4.19
.

The Cartesian diagram of Proposition
4.17
is the composition of Cartesian diagrams involving each
T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}
separately as
\mathfrak{p}
varies in
\Upsilon_{F,p}
. For each
\mathfrak{p}
, the corresponding Cartesian diagram is

\textstyle{Y_{H}(uK_{\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}}u^{-1}\cap H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm pr}^{\prime}\circ u\circ\iota_{\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{(\mathfrak{p})}\cdot K_{\mathfrak{p},r_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y_{H}(uK_{\boldsymbol{r}}u^{-1}\cap H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u\circ\iota_{\boldsymbol{r}}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}}),}

where
\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}
is given by
r_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\prime}=r_{\mathfrak{q}}
for
\mathfrak{q}\neq\mathfrak{p}
and
r_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}=r_{\mathfrak{p}}+1
,
{\rm pr}^{\prime}
denotes the natural projection
Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}})\to Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{(\mathfrak{p})}\cdot K_{\mathfrak{p},r_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\prime})
, and vertical arrows are the natural degeneracy maps.

Assume now that
\boldsymbol{r}=\boldsymbol{0}
. As the level subgroups
K_{\boldsymbol{0}},K_{\boldsymbol{0}}^{\prime},
and
K_{\boldsymbol{1}}
agree with
K_{0},K_{0}^{\prime},
and
K_{1}
, we adopt the latter notation. Note that in this case the bottom square of the diagram of Proposition
4.17
is not Cartesian as the degree of
\pi_{0}^{H}:Y_{H}(uK_{1}u^{-1}\cap H)\to Y_{H}(K_{0}\cap H)
is strictly smaller than
\pi_{0}^{G}:Y_{G}(K_{0}^{\prime})\to Y_{G}(K_{0})
, as the next lemma shows.

Lemma 4.20
.

The map
\pi_{0}^{H}:Y_{H}(uK_{1}u^{-1}\cap H)\to Y_{H}(K_{0}\cap H)
has degree
\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}(q_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}-q_{\mathfrak{p}})
and
\pi_{0}^{G}:Y_{G}(K_{0}^{\prime})\to Y_{G}(K_{0})
has degree
\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}q_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}
, where
q_{\mathfrak{p}}
is the cardinality of the residue field
\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}
of
\mathfrak{p}
.

Proof.

We first note that, as in the proof of Proposition
4.17
, we have

uK^{\prime}_{0}u^{-1}\cap H=uK_{1}u^{-1}\cap H.

Thus, by Lemma
4.18
, the degree of
\pi_{0}^{H}
equals to
[K_{0}\cap H:uK^{\prime}_{0}u^{-1}\cap H]
. To calculate the latter, since
u
is trivial outside
p
, it is enough to do the calculation prime by prime above
p
.

If
\mathfrak{p}
is a prime of
F
above
p
which is split in
E
, the component at
\mathfrak{p}
of
K_{0}\cap H
is given by
\Gamma_{\mathrm{GL}_{2},0}(\pi_{\mathfrak{p}})=\{g={{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}})\,:\,\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}\mid c\}
, while the one of
uK^{\prime}_{0}u^{-1}\cap H
is given by matrices
g={{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}})
with
\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}\mid c
,
\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}\mid b
, and
\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}\mid a-d
(
cf
. proof of Lemma
4.2
). The latter subgroup has index
(q_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)q_{\mathfrak{p}}
in
\Gamma_{\mathrm{GL}_{2},0}(\pi_{\mathfrak{p}})
.

Similarly, when
v
is inert in
E
the image of
uK_{0}^{\prime}u^{-1}\cap H
in
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{q_{\mathfrak{p}}})
is the center, implying that the
\mathfrak{p}
-component of
uK_{0}^{\prime}u^{-1}\cap H
has index in
\Gamma_{\mathrm{GL}_{2},0}(\pi_{\mathfrak{p}})
equal to
q_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}-q_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
Thus we have that
\pi_{0}^{H}
has degree
\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}(q_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}-q_{\mathfrak{p}})
.

The degree of
\pi_{0}^{G}
, by Lemma
4.18
, equals to
[K_{0}:K_{0}^{\prime}]
. Since the
p
-component of
K_{0}^{\prime}
is
\Gamma_{0}^{0}(p,p)
, its
\mathfrak{p}
-component has index in the Iwahori subgroup of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}})^{2}
for
\mathfrak{p}
split, resp.
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
for
\mathfrak{p}
inert, equal to
q_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}
. Thus, we have that
[K_{0}:K_{0}^{\prime}]=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}q_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}
.
∎

Note the difference of the two degrees at each prime
\mathfrak{p}
above
p
is
q_{\mathfrak{p}}
, which is exactly the index of the Hecke correspondence
U_{\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}}
at
\mathfrak{p}
associated with
[\Gamma^{0}_{H}(\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}){{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\Gamma^{0}_{H}(\pi_{\mathfrak{p}})]
, with
\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}
the uniformizer in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}
. A partial explanation of this phenomenon is given by the following proposition. Before that, we need to introduce some notation.

Let
\mathfrak{S}
be a subset of
\Upsilon_{F,p}
. Let
u_{\mathfrak{S}}\in G({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
denote the element
(\gamma_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}
given by

\gamma_{\mathfrak{p}}=\begin{cases}u_{\mathfrak{p}}&\text{ if }\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{S},\\
1&\text{ if }\mathfrak{p}\not\in\mathfrak{S},\end{cases}

with
u_{\mathfrak{p}}
given in Definition
4.1
.
Moreover, define

K^{0}_{H}(\mathfrak{S}):=(K^{(p)}\cap H(\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}^{(p)}))\cdot(u_{\mathfrak{S}}\Gamma^{0}_{0}(p,p)u_{\mathfrak{S}}^{-1}\cap H({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})),

where recall that
\Gamma^{0}_{0}(p,p)
is the
p
-component of
K_{0}^{\prime}
.
Finally, denote by
\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S}}:Y_{H}(K^{0}_{H}(\mathfrak{S}))\to Y_{G}(K_{0}^{\prime})
the closed immersion induced by
u_{\mathfrak{S}}\circ\iota
. A few remarks are in order.

Remark 4.21
.

(1)

For every
\mathfrak{p}\not\in\mathfrak{S}
, the
\mathfrak{p}
-component of
K^{0}_{H}(\mathfrak{S})
equals

\Gamma^{0}_{\mathrm{GL}_{2},0}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p})=\{h\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}})\,:\,h\in T_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}\,[\text{mod }\mathfrak{p}]\}.

(2)

If
\mathfrak{S}=\Upsilon_{F,p}
, we have
K^{0}_{H}(\mathfrak{S})=uK_{0}^{\prime}u^{-1}\cap H=uK_{1}u^{-1}\cap H
.

(3)

As the index of
\Gamma_{\mathrm{GL}_{2},0}^{0}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p})
in
\Gamma_{\mathrm{GL}_{2},0}(\mathfrak{p})
is
q_{\mathfrak{p}}
, an immediate generalization of Lemma
4.20
shows that the natural projection
{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S}}^{H}:Y_{H}(K^{0}_{H}(\mathfrak{S}))\to Y_{H}(K_{0}\cap H)
has degree equal to

\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\not\in\mathfrak{S}}q_{\mathfrak{p}}\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{S}}(q_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}-q_{\mathfrak{p}}).

The following Lemma is needed in the proof of Proposition
4.23
and makes explicit the open and closed
H
-orbits on the flag
\mathcal{F}
given in Lemma
4.2
.

Lemma 4.22
.

(1)

Let
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}
and consider the map

t_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\times\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}),\,g={{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\mapsto(g,g)u_{\mathfrak{p}}=\left({{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&a+b\\
c&c+d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}},{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\right).

Then the open orbit of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
acting on
\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\times\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
consists of points
(z_{1},z_{2})
with
z_{1}\neq z_{2}
and is realized as
t_{\mathfrak{p}}(\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))\cdot([0:1]^{T},[0:1]^{T})
, while the closed one consists of points
(z,z)
and equals
\iota_{\mathfrak{p}}(\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))\cdot([0:1]^{T},[0:1]^{T})
.

(2)

Let
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}
and let
\mathfrak{P}
be the only prime in
E
above
\mathfrak{p}
. Consider the map

t_{\mathfrak{p}}:\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\to\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{P}}),\,g={{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\mapsto gu_{\mathfrak{p}}={{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&a\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}+b\\
c&c\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}+d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}.

Then the open orbit of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
acting on
\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{P}})
consists of points
z\in\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{P}})\backslash\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{{\mathfrak{p}}})
and is realized as
t_{\mathfrak{p}}(\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))\cdot[0:1]^{T}
while the closed one consists of
z\in\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{{\mathfrak{p}}})
and equals
\iota_{\mathfrak{p}}(\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))\cdot[0:1]^{T}
.

Proof.

We start with the split case.
Recall that
\mathbb{P}^{1}=\mathrm{GL}_{2}/\overline{B}_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}
. The action of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
is given by left multiplication:

{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}[x:y]^{T}=[ax+by:cx+dy]^{T}.

So we have that the open orbit is

\left({{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&a+b\\
c&c+d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}},{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\right)([0:1]^{T},[0:1]^{T})=([a+b:c+d]^{T},[b:d]^{T})

and the closed one is

\left({{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}},{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\right)([0:1]^{T},[0:1]^{T})=([b:d]^{T},[b:d]^{T})

In the inert case we have the open orbit

{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&a\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}+b\\
c&c\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}+d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}[0:1]^{T}=[a\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}+b:c\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}+d]^{T}.

One sees that the
\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}
-coefficient of the ratio
a\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}+b/(c\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}+d)
is
0
if and only if
ad-bc=0
, which is never the case.
The closed orbit is instead

{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}[0:1]^{T}=[b:d]^{T}.

∎

We can finally state the equivalent of Proposition
4.17
for when
r=0
.

Proposition 4.23
.

Assume that
K^{(p)}
is stable under
\tau\in{\rm Gal}(E/F)
. The commutative diagram

\textstyle{\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{S}}Y_{H}(K^{0}_{H}(\mathfrak{S}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{({\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S}}^{H})_{\mathfrak{S}}}
\scriptstyle{(\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S}})_{\mathfrak{S}}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{0}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{0}^{G}}
\textstyle{Y_{H}(K_{0}\cap H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{0}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{0})}

is Cartesian. Moreover, it can be decomposed as a series of Cartesian diagrams indexed by the primes in
\Upsilon_{F,p}
: if
\mathfrak{S}
is a (possibly empty) subset of
\Upsilon_{F,p}
and
\mathfrak{p}\not\in\mathfrak{S}
, then, denoting
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}:=\mathfrak{S}\cup\{\mathfrak{p}\}
,

\textstyle{Y_{H}(u_{\mathfrak{S}}K_{0}(\mathfrak{S}^{\prime})u_{\mathfrak{S}}^{-1}\cap H)\sqcup Y_{H}(u_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}}K_{0}(\mathfrak{S}^{\prime})u_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}}^{-1}\cap H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{({\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S},\emptyset}^{H},{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S},\mathfrak{p}}^{H})}
\scriptstyle{(\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S}},u_{\mathfrak{p}}\circ\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S}})}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{G}}
\textstyle{Y_{H}(u_{\mathfrak{S}}K_{0}(\mathfrak{S})u_{\mathfrak{S}}^{-1}\cap H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S}}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{S})),}

is Cartesian, where
K_{0}(\mathfrak{S})
is the level subgroup introduced in (
13
).

Proof.

We mimic the proof of Proposition
4.17
. By Remark
4.21
(3), the degree of the map
({\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S}}^{H})_{\mathfrak{S}}
equals to

\sum_{\mathfrak{S}}[\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\not\in\mathfrak{S}}q_{\mathfrak{p}}\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{S}}(q_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}-q_{\mathfrak{p}})]=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}q_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2},

where the last equality follows from the binomial theorem. This implies that the degree of
({\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S}}^{H})_{\mathfrak{S}}
equals to the degree of
\pi_{0}^{G}
. To prove the result we are left to show that

\textstyle{\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{S}}Y_{H}(K^{0}_{H}(\mathfrak{S}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S}})_{\mathfrak{S}}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{0}^{\prime})}

is a closed immersion, where recall that
\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S}}=u_{\mathfrak{S}}\circ\iota
. This follows from the fact that each
\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S}}
is a closed immersion and that the images of any two sub-varieties do not intersect in
Y_{G}(K_{0}^{\prime})
.
The first fact is clear (
cf
. Lemma
4.15
), so we are left to show that the images don’t intersect. This follows roughly from the fact that the number of orbits of
H
in the flag
\mathcal{F}=G/\overline{B}_{G}
is in bijection with the subsets
\mathfrak{S}
of
\Upsilon_{F,p}
, with the open orbit corresponding to the choice of
\mathfrak{S}=\Upsilon_{F,p}
.
Precisely, note that the fiber of the natural degeneracy map
\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{G}
on the right can be identified with

\Gamma_{0}(p)/\Gamma_{0}^{0}(p,p)\cong B_{G}(\mathbb{F}_{p})/T_{G}(\mathbb{F}_{p})\cong N_{G}(\mathbb{F}_{p})\cong\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}}\left(N_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\times N_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\right)\times\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}}N_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{P}}),

for
\mathfrak{P}
the only prime in
E
above
\mathfrak{p}
. Moreover, as
N_{G}
can be realized as an open affine of
\mathcal{F}
, we can view the right hand side explicitly as the subspace of
\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}}\left(\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\times\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\right)\times\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}}\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{P}})
given by

\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}}\{\left([a:1],[a^{\prime}:1]\right),\,a,a^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}\}\times\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}}\{[b:1],\,b\in\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{P}}\}\,(=:\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}}N_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm s}\times\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}}N_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm i}).

We are left to show that the
\mathfrak{p}
-component of any element in the image of
Y_{H}(K_{0}^{H}(\mathfrak{S}))
is characterized by landing into the closed, resp. open, orbit for
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
when
\mathfrak{p}\not\in\mathfrak{S}
, resp.
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{S}
. This follows from our definition of
u_{\mathfrak{S}}
and the explicit calculation of Lemma
4.22
: indeed, when
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{S}
, the image of the
\mathfrak{p}
-component of
\Gamma_{H,0}(p)
into the
\mathfrak{p}
-component of
\Gamma_{0}(p)/\Gamma_{0}^{0}(p,p)
equals to
t_{\mathfrak{p}}(B_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))
, which realizes the intersection of the open orbit with
N_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm s}
, resp.
N_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm i}
, if
\mathfrak{p}
is split, resp. inert. When
\mathfrak{p}\not\in\mathfrak{S}
, it equals to
\iota_{\mathfrak{p}}(B_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))
, which gives the intersection of the closed orbit with
N_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm s}
, resp.
N_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\rm i}
, if
\mathfrak{p}
is split, resp. inert.
Therefore, if
\mathfrak{S},\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}
are two distinct subsets, then the images of
Y_{H}(K^{0}_{H}(\mathfrak{S}))
and
Y_{H}(K^{0}_{H}(\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}))
do not intersect, which proves our first claim. The second part of the statement follows in the same way.
∎

The interested reader might wonder what happens if we build our tower of level subgroups, starting from spherical level at
p
instead of
\Gamma_{0}(p)
, as done in
[
32
]
. The resulting Cartesian diagrams, which are almost identical to those given above, can be used to relate the cycles with spherical level at
p
with the twisted cycles in the
p
-tower of level subgroups. While the diagram of Proposition
4.17
translates verbatim, a few small modifications are required in the
r=0
case. We conclude this section by illustrating it. Let us denote

•

K_{{\rm un},0}:=K^{(p)}\cdot G({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
,
K_{{\rm un},0}^{\prime}:=K^{(p)}\cdot\Gamma^{0}(p)
;

•

for any subset
\mathfrak{S}
of
\Upsilon_{F,p}
,

K^{0}_{{\rm un},H}(\mathfrak{S}):=(K^{(p)}\cap H(\hat{{\mathbb{Z}}}^{(p)}))\cdot(u_{\mathfrak{S}}\Gamma^{0}(p)u_{\mathfrak{S}}^{-1}\cap H({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})).

Lemma 4.24
.

The natural degeneracy map
{\rm pr}_{{\rm un},\mathfrak{S}}^{H}:Y_{H}(K^{0}_{{\rm un},H}(\mathfrak{S}))\to Y_{H}(K_{un,0}\cap H)
has degree

\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\not\in\mathfrak{S}}(q_{\mathfrak{p}}+1)\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{S}\cap\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}}(q_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}+q_{\mathfrak{p}})\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{S}\cap\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}}q_{\mathfrak{p}}(q_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)

and the natural projection
\pi_{{\rm un},0}^{G}:Y_{G}(K_{{\rm un},0}^{\prime})\to Y_{G}(K_{{\rm un},0})
has degree
\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}}(q_{\mathfrak{p}}+1)^{2}\times\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}}(q^{2}_{\mathfrak{p}}+1)

Proof.

By Lemma
4.18
, the degree of
{\rm pr}_{{\rm un},\mathfrak{S}}^{H}
equals to
[K_{{\rm un},0}\cap H:K^{0}_{{\rm un},H}(\mathfrak{S})]
. To calculate the latter, since
u_{\mathfrak{S}}
is trivial outside
p
, it is enough to do the calculation prime by prime above
p
.

•

If
\mathfrak{p}
is a prime of
F
above
p
not in
\mathfrak{S}
, the
\mathfrak{p}
-component of
K^{0}_{{\rm un},H}(\mathfrak{S})
equals to
\Gamma^{0}_{\mathrm{GL}_{2}}(\mathfrak{p})
, which has index
q_{\mathfrak{p}}+1
in
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}})
.

•

If
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{S}
is split in
E
, the
\mathfrak{p}
-component of
K^{0}_{{\rm un},H}(\mathfrak{S})
is given by matrices
g={{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}})
with
\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}\mid b
and
\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}\mid a-d-c
(
cf
. proof of Lemma
4.2
). This subgroup has index
(q_{\mathfrak{p}}+1)q_{\mathfrak{p}}
in
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}})
.

•

If
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{S}
is inert in
E
, then the image of the
\mathfrak{p}
-component of
K^{0}_{{\rm un},H}(\mathfrak{S})
in
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{q_{\mathfrak{p}}})
is the non-split torus
\mathbb{F}_{q^{2}_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}
which has index
q_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}-q_{\mathfrak{p}}
in
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{q_{\mathfrak{p}}})
.

The degree of
\pi_{0}^{G}
, by Lemma
4.18
, equals to
[K_{{\rm un},0}:K_{{\rm un},0}^{\prime}]
. Since the
p
-component of a matrix in
K_{{\rm un},0}^{\prime}
is characterized by having lower left entry divisible by
p
and the index of the Iwahori subgroup of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}})^{2}
for
\mathfrak{p}
split, resp.
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{P}})
for
\mathfrak{p}
inert, is
(q_{\mathfrak{p}}+1)^{2}
, resp.
q_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}+1
, we have that
[K_{{\rm un},0}:K_{{\rm un},0}^{\prime}]=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}}(q_{\mathfrak{p}}+1)^{2}\times\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}}(q^{2}_{\mathfrak{p}}+1)
.
∎

Remark 4.25
.

If
\mathfrak{S}=\Upsilon_{F,p}
, the difference of the two degrees at each prime
\mathfrak{p}
above
p
is
q_{\mathfrak{p}}+1
, which is exactly the index of the correspondence for
T_{\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}}
, which is the Hecke operator at
v
given by
[H(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}){{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}H(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}})]
, with
\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}
the uniformizer in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}
. This is coherent with what we found above in Lemma
4.20
, where the difference was instead given by the degree of the correspondence
U_{\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}}
.

As before, denote by
\tilde{\iota}_{{\rm un},\mathfrak{S}}:Y_{H}(K^{0}_{{\rm un},H}(\mathfrak{S}))\to Y_{G}(K_{{\rm un},0}^{\prime})
the closed immersion induced by
u_{\mathfrak{S}}\circ\iota
.

Proposition 4.26
.

Assume that
K^{(p)}
is stable under
\tau\in{\rm Gal}(E/F)
. The commutative diagram

\textstyle{\bigsqcup_{\mathfrak{S}}Y_{H}(K^{0}_{{\rm un},H}(\mathfrak{S}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{({\rm pr}_{{\rm un},\mathfrak{S}}^{H})_{\mathfrak{S}}}
\scriptstyle{(\tilde{\iota}_{{\rm un},\mathfrak{S}})_{\mathfrak{S}}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{{\rm un},0}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{{\rm un},0}^{G}}
\textstyle{Y_{H}(K_{{\rm un},0}\cap H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{{\rm un},0}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{{\rm un},0})}

is Cartesian.

Proof.

We closely follow the proof of Proposition
4.23
. By Lemma
4.24
, using the binomial theorem we see that the degree of the map
({\rm pr}_{{\rm un},\mathfrak{S}}^{H})_{\mathfrak{S}}
equals to

\sum_{\mathfrak{S}}[\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\not\in\mathfrak{S}}(q_{\mathfrak{p}}+1)\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{S}\cap\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}}(q_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}+q_{\mathfrak{p}})\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{S}\cap\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}}q_{\mathfrak{p}}(q_{\mathfrak{p}}-1)]=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}}(q_{\mathfrak{p}}+1)^{2}\times\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}}(q^{2}_{\mathfrak{p}}+1).

This implies that the degree of the two vertical maps coincide. We are left to show that the upper horizontal map is a closed immersion. Since each
\tilde{\iota}_{{\rm un},\mathfrak{S}}
is, we are left to show that the images of any the two immersions do not intersect in
Y_{G}(K_{{\rm un},0}^{\prime})
.

Note that the fiber of the right vertical map can be identified with

G({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})/\Gamma^{0}(p)\cong G(\mathbb{F}_{p})/\overline{B}_{G}(\mathbb{F}_{p})\cong\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm s}_{F,p}}\left(\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\times(\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})\right)\times\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon^{\rm i}_{F,p}}\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{P}}),

for
\mathfrak{P}
the only prime in
E
above
\mathfrak{p}
.
As in Proposition
4.23
, we are thus left to show that the
\mathfrak{p}
-component of any element in the image of
Y_{H}(K^{0}_{{\rm un},H}(\mathfrak{S}))
is characterized by landing into the closed, resp. open, orbit for
\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}})
when
\mathfrak{p}\not\in\mathfrak{S}
, resp.
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{S}
. This follows again from Lemma
4.22
: when
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{S}
, the image of the
\mathfrak{p}
-component of
H({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
into the
\mathfrak{p}
-component of
G({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})/\Gamma^{0}(p)
equals to
t_{\mathfrak{p}}(\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))
, which realizes the open orbit. When
\mathfrak{p}\not\in\mathfrak{S}
, it equals to
\iota_{\mathfrak{p}}({\mathrm{GL}_{2}}(\mathbb{F}_{\mathfrak{p}}))
, which is the closed orbit.
Therefore, if
\mathfrak{S},\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}
are two distinct subsets, then the images of
\tilde{\iota}_{{\rm un},\mathfrak{S}}
and
\tilde{\iota}_{{\rm un},\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}}
are disjoint.
∎

4.4.
Moment maps and specialisation

For
r\geq 1
, let
V_{r}=K^{(p)}\cdot V_{p,r}
denote the level subgroup of Definition
4.7
. Consider a big enough set of primes
S
containing
p
and every prime
\ell
such that
K^{(p)}_{\ell}
is not hyperspecial. By abuse of notation, we denote by
Y_{G}(V_{r})
the integral model of the Shimura variety for
G
over
{\mathbb{Z}}[1/S]
. Recall we have fixed a prime
\mathfrak{m}
of the coefficient field
L
above
p
. Consider, for any integer
i
, the groups

H^{i}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}):=\varprojlim_{r\geq 1}H^{i}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}),

where the inverse limit is taken with respect to the trace maps associated with the natural degeneracy maps
{\rm pr}_{r+1,r}:Y_{G}(V_{r+1})\to Y_{G}(V_{r})
.

Remark 4.27
.

Recall that we have defined
[\eta]_{r+1,r}:Y_{G}(K_{r+1})\to Y_{G}(K_{r})
in Definition
4.11
. Moreover, we have a normalized trace map
\eta^{r}_{\ast}
of
\eta^{r}:Y_{G}(K_{r})\to Y_{G}(V_{r})
defined as in Lemma
4.10
as the composition of
\eta_{\flat}^{r}
with the pushforward of
\eta^{r}
. Note that we have an equality

\eta^{r}_{\ast}\circ{\rm Tr}_{[\eta]_{r+1,r}}={\rm pr}_{r+1,r,\ast}\circ\eta^{r+1}_{\ast},

induced by the commutative diagram

\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{r+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{r+1}}
\scriptstyle{[\eta]_{r+1,r}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(V_{r+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm pr}_{r+1,r}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{r})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{r}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(V_{r}).}

We let
\overline{\mathcal{E}}
be the closure of the totally positive units in
T_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, and
{\mathcal{E}}(p^{r})
the image of the totally positive units in
T_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{r})
. We write

(19)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{G/H}:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}\llbracket T_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})/\overline{\mathcal{E}}L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\rrbracket\simeq\varprojlim_{r}\Lambda_{G/H,r}\,\left(:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}^{r}[T_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{r})/{\mathcal{E}}(p^{r})L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{r})]\right),

where
L_{G}=\{{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\alpha&\\
&\tau(\alpha)\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\,:\,\alpha\in{\rm Res}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}/{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathbf{G}_{\rm m}\}
.

The Iwasawa cohomology groups defined above have naturally a continuous action of the quotient
T_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})/\overline{\mathcal{E}}L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, which makes them into Iwasawa modules over the Iwasawa algebra
\Lambda_{G/H}
.

Notice that the kernel of the map
T_{H}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\rightarrow T_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})/L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
is exactly the center of
H({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
and thus we have an action of the Iwasawa algebra
\Lambda_{G/\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}\llbracket T_{H}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})/Z_{H}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\rrbracket}
on these cohomology groups. The Hecke eigensystems that appear in
H^{i}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})
correspond to automorphic representations with trivial central character, (
i.e.
the Nebentypus is trivial and
m=0
).

We need to construct specialization maps from
H^{i}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})
to
H^{i}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})
, which will be used to relate the universal Hirzebruch–Zagier cycle with (the ordinary projection of) the cycle studied by Getz–Goresky.
Following
[
31
]
, the key input is a vector in
\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}
which is invariant under
V_{r}
modulo
\mathfrak{m}^{r}
, and that has the same ordinary projection as the
u
-translate of the
H(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})
-invariant vector
\Delta^{\underline{k}}
. This motivates the switch from the tower of level subgroups
(K_{r})_{r}
to
(V_{r})_{r}
instead.

Let
v_{\underline{k}}^{\rm hw}
be the highest weight vector for
V^{\underline{k},0}
. As
V_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\underline{k},0}
is admissible, its intersection with the highest weight subspace of
V^{\underline{k},0}
is
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot v_{\underline{k}}^{\rm hw}
. For any
r\geq 1
, denote by
v_{\underline{k},r}^{\rm hw}
the reduction modulo
\mathfrak{m}^{r}
.

Lemma 4.28
.

Let
\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Sigma_{E}}
be such that
k_{\sigma}=k_{\sigma^{\prime}}
for every
\sigma\in\Phi
. For each
r\geq 1
,
v_{\underline{k},r}^{\rm hw}\in H^{0}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}^{r}})
. Moreover, we have that

v_{\underline{k},r+1}^{\rm hw}\,[{\rm mod}\,\,p^{r}]=({\rm pr}_{r+1,r})^{\ast}v_{\underline{k},r}^{\rm hw}\in H^{0}(Y_{G}(V_{r+1}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}^{r}}).

Proof.

By definition, the image of
V_{r}
in
G({\mathbb{Z}}/p^{r}{\mathbb{Z}})
sits inside
L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}/p^{r}{\mathbb{Z}})\cdot N_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}/p^{r}{\mathbb{Z}})
. Being
v_{\underline{k},r}^{\rm hw}
the highest weight vector,
N_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}/p^{r}{\mathbb{Z}})
acts trivially on it. Moreover,
L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}/p^{r}{\mathbb{Z}})
acts on the highest weight vector
v_{\underline{k},r}^{\rm hw}
of
\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},m}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}^{r}}
exactly via
\mathrm{N}^{m}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}
[mod
\mathfrak{m}^{r}
]. Thus, it defines an element of
H^{0}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}^{r}})
.

For the second statement, this simply follows from reducing modulo
\mathfrak{m}^{r}
the vector
v_{\underline{k},r+1}^{\rm hw}
, which is naturally
({\rm pr}_{r+1,r})^{\ast}v_{\underline{k},r}^{\rm hw}
, where
({\rm pr}_{r+1,r})^{\ast}
is just the restriction of the
V_{r}
-representation
V^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}^{r}}
to
V_{r+1}
.
∎

Thanks to the lemma, we can make the following (
cf
.
[
31
, Definition 5.1.1]
).

Definition 4.29
.

Let
\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Sigma_{E}}
be such that
k_{\sigma}=k_{\sigma^{\prime}}
for every
\sigma\in\Phi
. For
\boldsymbol{r}=(r_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{p}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}^{|\Upsilon_{F,p}|}
, we define the moment map

\mathrm{mom}^{\underline{k}}_{\boldsymbol{r}}:H^{i}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})\rightarrow H^{i}(Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})

in the following way:

\displaystyle\mathrm{mom}^{\underline{k}}_{\boldsymbol{r}}:H^{i}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{}\varprojlim_{s}H^{i}(Y_{G}(V_{s}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/{\mathfrak{m}^{s}})

\displaystyle\to\varprojlim_{s}H^{i}(Y_{G}(V_{s}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}^{s}})

\displaystyle\to\varprojlim_{s}H^{i}(Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}^{s}})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{i}(Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})

where the first map is the reduction modulo
\mathfrak{m}^{s}
in the projective system, the second map is cup product
\bullet\cup v_{\underline{k},s}^{\rm hw}

\in H^{0}(Y_{G}(V_{s}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}^{s}})
, the third one is the projection to level
\boldsymbol{r}
, and the last one is an isomorphism.

When
\boldsymbol{r}=(r,\dots,r)
, we denote the moment map by
\mathrm{mom}^{\underline{k}}_{r}
.

4.5.
Control theorem for Iwasawa cohomology

We show that the ordinary part of our Iwasawa cohomology is of finite type over the Iwasawa algebra
\Lambda_{G/H}
, and a control theorem. We note that similar results have been obtained in
[
40
]
.

Lemma 4.30
.

For
r\geq 1
, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{H^{i}(Y_{G}(V_{r+1}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{\eta}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm pr}_{r+1,r,\ast}}
\textstyle{H^{i}(Y_{G}(V_{r+1}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm pr}_{r+1,r,\ast}}
\textstyle{\!H^{i}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{\eta}}
\textstyle{H^{i}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}),}

where the diagonal map is the natural restriction map induced by the pullback of the projection
Y_{G}(V_{r+1})\xrightarrow{{\rm pr}}Y_{G}(\eta^{-r-1}K_{r}\eta^{r+1})\xrightarrow{\eta^{-1}}Y_{G}(V_{r})
, which is normalized integrally as in Lemma
4.10
.

Proof.

This lemma is the well known fact (already known to Shimura) that
T_{p}
lowers the level at
p
. One follows essentially the proof of
[
20
, (8.8)]
: one calculates the explicit coset decomposition of
T_{\eta}
and the explicit description of the trace of
{\rm pr}_{r+1,r}
to check they are the same.
∎

Lemma 4.31
.

There exists an ordinary projector
e^{{\rm n.ord}}
on
H^{i}(Y_{G}(U),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})

such that

e^{\rm n.ord}H^{i}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})=\varprojlim_{r}e^{\rm n.ord}H^{i}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}).

Proof.

The existence of the idempotent on
H^{i}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})
is well-known, see for example
[
24
, §1.11]
that is, one considers the direct limit of
T_{\eta}^{n!}
, for
n
going to infinity. The key fact is that
T_{\eta}
is optimally normalized to preserve the integral structure, see Remark
4.12
.

The idempotent on
H^{i}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})
is defined as the inverse limit over
r
of the idempotent at each level.
∎

Let
K_{0}(p^{r})
be the congruence subgroup given by
K^{(p)}\cdot\Gamma_{0}(p^{r})
. Observe that, by Remark
4.8
,
K_{0}(p^{2})=V_{0}^{\prime}
. Consider
the sheaf on
Y_{G}(K_{0}(p^{2}))
given by
D_{r}:=s_{r,\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}
, where
s_{r}:Y_{G}(V_{r})\to Y_{G}(K_{0}(p^{2}))
denotes the natural projection map. Observe that
D_{r}
splits in a unipotent part
\Lambda_{\overline{N}_{G,r}}
and a toric part
\Lambda_{G/H,r}
; note that this decomposition is
K_{0}(p^{r+1})
-equivariant but not
K_{0}(p^{2})
-equivariant. Here,
\overline{N}_{G,r}
denotes the quotient of
K_{0}(p^{2})\cap\overline{N}_{G}({{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}})
with
K_{0}(p^{r+1})\cap\overline{N}_{G}({{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}})
.

We then define

H^{i}(Y_{G}(K_{0}(p^{2})),D):=\varprojlim_{r}H^{i}(Y_{G}(K_{0}(p^{2})),D_{r}).

We have an isomorphism
D\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}\llbracket\overline{N}_{G}\rrbracket\hat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\Lambda_{G/H}
, where
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}\llbracket\overline{N}_{G}\rrbracket=\varprojlim_{r}\Lambda_{\overline{N}_{G,r}}
.

Proposition 4.32
.

We have an isomorphism of
\Lambda_{G/H}
-modules

e^{\rm n.ord}H^{i}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})\cong e^{\rm n.ord}H^{i}(Y_{G}(K_{0}(p^{2})),D)=:e^{\rm n.ord}H^{i}(Y_{G}(K_{0}(p^{2})),\Lambda_{G/H}).

Remark 4.33
.

The reason why we call the right hand side
e^{\rm n.ord}H^{i}(Y_{G}(K_{0}(p^{2})),\Lambda_{G/H})
is because the ordinary projector
e^{\rm n.ord}
kills the contribution of the lower unipotent part
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}\llbracket\overline{N}_{G}\rrbracket
, see
[
24
, (2.5a), p.280 ]
.

Proof.

Recall we have
H^{i}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})=\varprojlim_{r\geq 1}H^{i}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})
where the transition maps are essentially given by
T_{\eta}
by Lemma
4.30
. Moreover,

e^{\rm n.ord}H^{i}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})=\varprojlim_{n}e^{\rm n.ord}H^{i}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}^{n}).

Note that, by Remark
4.8
,
K_{0}(p^{r+1})
contains
V_{r}
as a normal subgroup and the quotient is isomorphic to
T_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{r})/L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{r})
. In order to use Shapiro lemma, first we note that the cohomology of
H^{i}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})
can be calculated in terms of group cohomology of several congruence subgroups
\overline{\Gamma_{i}}
of
{\rm PGL}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{E})
, see
[
20
, (6.4)]
(assuming the
\overline{\Gamma_{i}}
are torsion free). As
{\mathcal{E}}(p^{r})
acts trivially on the corresponding locally symmetric space, by Shapiro lemma we have

e^{\rm n.ord}H^{i}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}^{n})\cong e^{\rm n.ord}H^{i}(Y_{G}(K_{0}(p^{r+1})),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}^{n}[T_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{r})/{\mathcal{E}}(p^{r})L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{r})]).

As applying the ordinary projector kills the contribution by
\Lambda_{\overline{N}_{G,r}}

[
24
, (2.5a)]
,
the latter in turn is isomorphic to

e^{\rm n.ord}H^{i}(Y_{G}(K_{0}(p^{r+1})),D_{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}^{n}).

By a variation of Lemma
4.30
to lower the level at
p
, this is also isomorphic to

e^{\rm n.ord}H^{i}(Y_{G}(K_{0}(p^{2})),D_{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}^{n}).

The result follows from this and the definition of the module

e^{\rm n.ord}H^{i}(Y_{G}(K_{0}(p^{2})),\Lambda_{G/H})
.
∎

We let
\mathcal{H}_{\rm Iw}
be the (big) Hecke algebra acting on
H^{i}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})
, generated by the (spherical) Hecke operators
T_{v}
,
S_{v}
at each place
v
where
V_{r}
is hyperspecial, and by
T_{\eta}
.

Theorem 4.34
.

Let
\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Sigma_{E}}
be such that
k_{\sigma}=k_{\sigma^{\prime}}
for all
\sigma\in\Phi
and let
\mathfrak{m}_{f}
be a maximal ideal of the Hecke algebra of
e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})
and suppose that it lifts
7
7

7

This always holds if
\mathfrak{m}_{f}
is a maximal ideal of residual characteristic
0
supported only in parabolic cohomology - see
[
2
, Theorem 2.7]
to an ideal of
\mathcal{H}_{\rm Iw}
. If

e^{\rm n.ord}H^{\bullet}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})_{\mathfrak{m}_{f}}\cong e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})_{\mathfrak{m}_{f}},

and the cohomology is torsion free, then the specialisation map

e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})\otimes_{\Lambda_{G/H}}\kappa_{f}\rightarrow e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\kappa_{f}}),

induced by the moment map in Definition
4.29
, is an isomorphism after localisation at
\mathfrak{m}_{f}
, where
\kappa_{f}
is the residual field of
\mathfrak{m}_{f}
.

Proof.

First, the moment map of Definition
4.29
induces a specialisation map

e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})\otimes_{\Lambda_{G/H}}\kappa_{f}\rightarrow e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\kappa_{f}}).

Now, note that we can use Proposition
4.32
to
get

e^{\rm n.ord}H^{i}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})\otimes_{\Lambda_{G/H}}\kappa_{f}\cong e^{\rm n.ord}H^{i}(Y_{G}(K_{0}(p^{2})),\Lambda_{G/H})\otimes_{\Lambda_{G/H}}\kappa_{f},

so we are left to study the tensor product on the right hand side. The default of the right hand side being isomorphic to
e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\kappa_{f}})
is controlled by a spectral sequence as in
[
31
, Cor. 2.7.11]
: after localization at
\mathfrak{m}_{f}
, the hypothesis that the
\mathfrak{m}_{f}
-part of the cohomology is concentrated in a single degree ensures that all the terms in
loc. cit.
vanish for
i\neq 0
, giving that the above map is an isomorphism.

∎

Remark 4.35
.

To apply this theorem in characteristic
p
, one needs to verify that the cohomology is concentrated in one degree. This is not easy and in general not true, for example if there are Eisenstein congruences. We refer to
[
8
]
for the state-of-the-art result.

5.
p
-adic families of twisted Hirzebruch–Zagier cycles

5.1.
Cycles at finite level in cohomology

Throughout the section, we assume that
K^{(p)}
is a neat open compact subgroup of
G({\mathbb{A}}_{f})
, which is stable under
\tau\in{\rm Gal}(E/F)
. Recall that we have the morphism

\textstyle{u\circ\iota_{\boldsymbol{r}}:Y_{H}(uK_{\boldsymbol{r}}u^{-1}\cap H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\boldsymbol{r}}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(uK_{\boldsymbol{r}}u^{-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}}),}

where
u\in G({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
denotes the element of Definition
4.1
. Thanks to Lemma
4.15
, we can define the pushforward map
u_{\star}\circ{\iota_{\boldsymbol{r}}}_{\star}
in cohomology. For a given local system
\mathscr{V}
on
Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}})
, we have

u_{\star}\circ{\iota_{\boldsymbol{r}}}_{\star}:H^{\bullet}(Y_{H}(uK_{\boldsymbol{r}}u^{-1}\cap H),\mathscr{V}_{|_{H}})\longrightarrow H^{\bullet+2d}(Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}}),\mathscr{V}).

Recall that, in §
2.3
, we have shown that there exists an
H
-equivariant map
\iota^{\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}\hookrightarrow V^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}

with
\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Sigma_{E}}
such that
k_{\sigma}=k_{\sigma^{\prime}}
for all
\sigma\in\Phi
. In view of Lemma
4.6
, we normalize it by letting

\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}\hookrightarrow V^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}

be the map which sends
1\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}
to
\Delta^{\dagger,\underline{k}}:=a_{\underline{k}}^{-1}\cdot\Delta^{\underline{k}}
, where
a_{\underline{k}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\times}
is the constant introduced in Lemma
4.6
.
The induced map in cohomology is

\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}:H^{0}(Y_{H}(U),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})\longrightarrow H^{0}(Y_{H}(U),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}|_{H}),

where
\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}
denotes the local system associated with
V^{\underline{k},0}
.
Note that the image of the characteristic class
\mathbf{1}_{Y_{H}(U)}
via
\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}
can be explicitly described by using the invariant vector
\Delta^{\dagger,\underline{k}}
(
cf
.
[
17
, Proposition 9.1]
).

Definition 5.1
.

Let
\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Sigma_{E}}
be such that
k_{\sigma}=k_{\sigma^{\prime}}
for all
\sigma\in\Phi
. The Hirzebruch–Zagier cycle
z_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\underline{k}}
of weight
\underline{k}
and level
K_{\boldsymbol{r}}
, with
\boldsymbol{r}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{|\Upsilon_{F,p}|}
, is defined as

z_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\underline{k}}:=u_{\star}\circ{\iota_{\boldsymbol{r}}}_{\star}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{Y_{H}(uK_{\boldsymbol{r}}u^{-1}\cap H)})\in H^{2d}(Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}).

We also define the twisted Hirzebruch–Zagier cycle
\mathscr{Z}_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\underline{k}}
of weight
\underline{k}
and level
V_{\boldsymbol{r}}
as

\mathscr{Z}_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\underline{k}}:=\eta^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\ast}\,z_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\underline{k}}\in H^{2d}(Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}),

where
\eta^{\boldsymbol{r}}:=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}}\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{r_{\mathfrak{p}}}
and
\eta^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\ast}
denotes the composition of all the traces of
\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{r_{\mathfrak{p}}}
normalized as in Remark
4.14
.

When
\boldsymbol{r}=(r,\dots,r)
, the level subgroups are the
K_{r}
and
V_{r}
of Definition
4.7
and we denote the corresponding classes by
\mathscr{Z}_{r}^{\underline{k}}
,
z_{r}^{\underline{k}}
.

Remark 5.2
.

When
\boldsymbol{r}=\boldsymbol{0}
, there is an overlap in the notation used as the levels
K_{0},V_{0}
and classes
\mathscr{Z}_{0}^{\underline{k}}
,
z_{0}^{\underline{k}}
both coincide.

5.2.
Universal cycles

Following
[
32
]
, we can use the results of §
4
to construct a universal family of Hirzebruch–Zagier cycles.

Proposition 5.3
.

For all
\boldsymbol{r}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}^{|\Upsilon_{F,p}|}
, we have

{\rm pr}_{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1},\boldsymbol{r},\ast}\left(\mathscr{Z}_{\boldsymbol{r}+1}^{\underline{k}}\right)=T_{\eta}\cdot\mathscr{Z}_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\underline{k}},

where recall that
{\rm pr}_{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1},\boldsymbol{r}}
denotes the natural degeneracy map
Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}})\to Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}})
.

Proof.

This is
[
32
, Proposition 4.5.1]
: by the Cartesian diagram of Proposition
4.17
, we have that

\displaystyle{\rm pr}_{\ast}z_{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}^{\underline{k}}=(\pi_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{G})^{\ast}z_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\underline{k}}.

Applying the trace of
\eta
to both sides, we get

(20)

\displaystyle{\rm Tr}_{[\eta]_{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1},\boldsymbol{r}}}(z_{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}^{\underline{k}})=T_{\eta}\cdot z_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\underline{k}}.

Note that the (integrally normalized) trace
\eta^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\ast}
satisfies the equality

\eta^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\ast}\circ{\rm Tr}_{[\eta]_{{\boldsymbol{r}}+{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{r}}}}={\rm pr}_{{\boldsymbol{r}}+{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{r}},\ast}\circ\eta^{{\boldsymbol{r}}+{\boldsymbol{1}}}_{\ast},

induced by the commutative diagram

\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{{\boldsymbol{r}}+{\boldsymbol{1}}}}
\scriptstyle{[\eta]_{{\boldsymbol{r}}+{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{r}}}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(V_{{\boldsymbol{r}}+{\boldsymbol{1}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm pr}_{{\boldsymbol{r}}+{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{r}}}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{\boldsymbol{r}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}}).}

This implies that, after applying
\eta^{{\boldsymbol{r}}}_{\ast}
to (
20
), we get

{\rm pr}_{{\boldsymbol{r}}+{\boldsymbol{1}},{\boldsymbol{r}},\ast}\left(\mathscr{Z}_{{\boldsymbol{r}}+{\boldsymbol{1}}}^{\underline{k}}\right)=\eta^{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\ast}\left(T_{\eta}\cdot z_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\underline{k}}\right)=T_{\eta}\cdot\mathscr{Z}_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\underline{k}}.

∎

Recall that we have the
H
-equivariant vector
\Delta^{\dagger,\underline{k}}=a_{\underline{k}}^{-1}\cdot\Delta^{\underline{k}}
in
V_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\underline{k},0}
,
with
\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Sigma_{E}}
such that
k_{\sigma}=k_{\sigma^{\prime}}
for all
\sigma\in\Phi
(
cf
. Lemma
2.9
). For any
r\geq 1
denote by
\Delta^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{r}
its reduction modulo
\mathfrak{m}^{r}
.

We have gathered all the ingredients to define the desired Hirzebruch–Zagier class:

Theorem 5.4
.

There is a Big Hirzebruch–Zagier class

\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}:=\varprojlim_{r}T_{\eta}^{-r}e^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}\mathscr{Z}_{r}^{\underline{0}}\in e^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}H^{2d}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}).

Let
\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Sigma_{E}}
be such that
k_{\sigma}=k_{\sigma^{\prime}}
for all
\sigma\in\Phi
. Then, for every
\boldsymbol{r}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}^{|\Upsilon_{F,p}|}
, we have

\mathrm{mom}^{\underline{k}}_{\boldsymbol{r}}\,\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}=T_{\eta}^{-\boldsymbol{r}}e^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}\,\mathscr{Z}_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\underline{k}},

with
T_{\eta}^{-\boldsymbol{r}}=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}}T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{-r_{\mathfrak{p}}}
.

Proof.

After applying the ordinary idempotent, by Proposition
5.3
the classes
T_{\eta}^{-r}e^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}\mathscr{Z}_{r}^{\underline{0}}
are compatible via the pushforward
{\rm pr}_{r+1,r,\ast}
for
r\geq 1
and thus they define an element (
cf
.
[
32
, Proposition 4.5.2]
)

\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}\in e^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}H^{2d}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}).

We now evaluate the moment map on
\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}
as in
[
31
, Theorem 5.2.1]
. For
\boldsymbol{r}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 1}^{|\Upsilon_{F,p}|}
, let
r={\rm max}_{\mathfrak{p}}\{r_{\mathfrak{p}}\}
and note that
\mathrm{mom}^{\underline{k}}_{\boldsymbol{r}}
factors as the composition of
\mathrm{mom}^{\underline{k}}_{r}
and the trace of the natural projection
{\rm pr}_{r,\boldsymbol{r}}:Y_{G}(V_{r})\to Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}})
. We start with showing that

(21)
