\displaystyle\mathrm{mom}^{\underline{k}}_{r}\,\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}=T_{\eta}^{-r}e^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}\,\mathscr{Z}_{r}^{\underline{k}}.

Unfolding Definition
4.29
, proving this follows from showing that

\mathscr{Z}_{r}^{\underline{k}}\,\text{mod }\mathfrak{m}^{r}=(\mathscr{Z}_{r}^{\underline{0}}\,\text{mod }\mathfrak{m}^{r})\cup v_{\underline{k},r}^{\rm hw}

as elements in
H^{2d}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}^{r}})
. Now, recall that
\mathscr{Z}_{r}^{\underline{k}}=\eta^{r}_{\ast}(z_{r}^{\underline{k}})
, where
\eta^{r}_{\ast}
is the composition of the pushforward by
\eta^{r}
and the morphism of local systems
\eta^{r}_{\flat}:\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}^{r}}\to(\eta^{r})^{\ast}\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}^{r}}
. Recall that
\eta^{r}_{\flat}
is associated with a map
V^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}^{r}}\to(\eta^{r})^{\ast}V^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathfrak{m}^{r}}
which is the identity on the highest weight subspace and acts by powers of
p^{r}
(hence zero) elsewhere (
cf
. Remark
4.12
). Thus, it suffices to show

z_{r}^{\underline{k}}\;\text{mod }\mathfrak{m}^{r}=(z_{r}^{\underline{0}}\;\text{mod }\mathfrak{m}^{r})\cup v_{\underline{k},r}^{\rm hw}\;\text{mod ker}\,\eta^{r}_{\flat}.

Using the projection formula, we can write the left hand side as

(z_{r}^{\underline{0}}\;\text{mod }\mathfrak{m}^{r})\cup u^{-1}\cdot\Delta^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{r},

thus the result follows from checking that
u^{-1}\cdot\Delta^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{r}
and
v_{\underline{k},r}^{\rm hw}
agree modulo
\text{ker}\,\eta^{r}_{\flat}
. As
\eta^{r}_{\flat}
kills everything outside the highest weight space, this follows from checking that the projection of
u^{-1}\cdot\Delta^{\underline{k}}
to the highest weight space of
V^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}
agrees with
v_{\underline{k}}^{\rm hw}
, up to the constant
a_{\underline{k}}
. This is immediate from the definition of
u
and the explicit realization of
\Delta^{\underline{k}}
given in Lemma
2.7
, as shown in Lemma
4.6
. This proves (
21
) and the result follows from showing that, if
\boldsymbol{s}=(r)_{\mathfrak{p}}-\boldsymbol{r}
, we have

{\rm pr}_{r,\boldsymbol{r},\star}\,\mathscr{Z}_{r}^{\underline{k}}=T_{\eta}^{\boldsymbol{s}}\mathscr{Z}_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\underline{k}}.

This follows, as in Proposition
5.3
, by using the Cartesian diagrams of Remark
4.19
recursively for each
\mathfrak{p}
with
\boldsymbol{s}_{\mathfrak{p}}\neq 0
.
∎

We note that, after allowing
p
-adic square roots of the determinant, one could also define another
p
-adic family of cycles interpolating Hirzebruch–Zagier cycles at odd weights and non-trivial central characters.

5.3.
Euler Factor on the First Floor

In what follows we relate the universal cycle to the Hirzebruch–Zagier cycles of level
V_{0}
.

Recall that, for each
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}
,
\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}:={\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(F_{\mathfrak{p}})
and we denote in the same way the corresponding element of
H({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. Note that the morphism
\eta_{K_{0}^{\prime},K_{0}}:Y_{G}(K_{0}^{\prime})\to Y_{G}(K_{0})
can be written as the composition of

Y_{G}(K_{0}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}\circ\eta_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}}}Y_{G}(K_{0}(\Upsilon_{F,p}\smallsetminus\{\mathfrak{p}_{1}\}))\xrightarrow{{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{p}_{2}}\circ\eta_{\mathfrak{p}_{2}}}Y_{G}(K_{0}(\Upsilon_{F,p}\smallsetminus\{\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2}\}))\cdots Y_{G}(K_{0}(\{\mathfrak{p}_{r}\}))\xrightarrow{{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{p}_{r}}\circ\eta_{\mathfrak{p}_{r}}}Y_{G}(K_{0}),

where we denoted
\Upsilon_{F,p}=\{\mathfrak{p}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{p}_{r}\}
and where, for any
\mathfrak{S}
subset of
\Upsilon_{F,p}
, we have set in (
13
)

K_{0}(\mathfrak{S})=K^{(p)}\cdot(\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{S}}\Gamma^{0}_{0}(\pi_{\mathfrak{p}},\pi_{\mathfrak{p}})\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}\smallsetminus\mathfrak{S}}\Gamma_{0}(\pi_{\mathfrak{p}})).

Recall (Remark
4.14
) that we have

T_{\eta}=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}},

where
T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is the Hecke operator acting on cohomology with coefficients
\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}
, normalized by multiplying the trace by
\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}
by
p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}k_{\sigma}}
when
k_{\sigma}=k_{\sigma^{\prime}}
for every
\sigma\in\Phi
.

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Sigma_{E}}
be such that
k_{\sigma}=k_{\sigma^{\prime}}
for every
\sigma\in\Phi
. We have

{\rm Tr}_{[\eta]_{1,0}}(z_{1}^{\underline{k}})=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}\left(T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}-q_{\mathfrak{p}}\cdot p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}k_{\sigma}}\right)\cdot z_{0}^{\underline{k}}\in H^{2d}(Y_{G}(K_{0}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}).

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{p}
be in
\Upsilon_{F,p}
. By Proposition
4.23
, denoting
\mathfrak{S}:=\Upsilon_{F,p}\smallsetminus\{\mathfrak{p}\}

we have the Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{Y_{H}(u_{\mathfrak{S}}K_{0}^{\prime}u_{\mathfrak{S}}^{-1}\cap H)\sqcup Y_{H}(uK_{0}^{\prime}u^{-1}\cap H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{({\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S},\emptyset}^{H},{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S},\mathfrak{p}}^{H})}
\scriptstyle{(\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S}},u\circ\iota)}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{0}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{G}}
\textstyle{Y_{H}(u_{\mathfrak{S}}K_{0}(\mathfrak{S})u_{\mathfrak{S}}^{-1}\cap H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S}}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{S})),}

where, recall that
K_{0}(\mathfrak{S})
, resp.
K_{0}^{\prime}
, has
\mathfrak{p}
-component
\Gamma_{0}(\pi_{\mathfrak{p}})
, resp.
\Gamma^{0}_{0}(\pi_{\mathfrak{p}},\pi_{\mathfrak{p}})
. From the Cartesian diagram, we obtain

(23)

\displaystyle{\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{G,*}}\circ\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S},*}=\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S},*}\circ{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S},\emptyset}^{H,*}+u_{*}\circ\iota_{*}\circ{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S},\mathfrak{p}}^{H,*}.

Apply (
23
) to
\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}}:=\mathbf{1}_{u_{\mathfrak{S}}K_{0}(\mathfrak{S})u_{\mathfrak{S}}^{-1}\cap H}
:

u_{*}\circ\iota_{*}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\circ{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S},\mathfrak{p}}^{H,*}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}})={\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{G,*}}\circ\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S},*}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}})-\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S},*}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\circ{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S},\emptyset}^{H,*}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}}).

Recall that
uK_{0}^{\prime}u^{-1}\cap H=uK_{1}u^{-1}\cap H
, hence, similarly to Proposition
5.3
, the equation above reads as

{\rm pr}_{K_{1},K_{0}^{\prime},\ast}(z_{1}^{\underline{k}})={\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{G,*}}\circ\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S},*}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}})-\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S},*}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\circ{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S},\emptyset}^{H,*}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}}).

We now apply the trace of
Y_{G}(K_{0}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{p}}\circ\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}Y_{G}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{S}))
, which we normalize, similarly to Lemma
4.10
, by multiplying it with
p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}k_{\sigma}}
. We get

\displaystyle({\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{p}}\circ\eta_{\mathfrak{p}})_{*}\circ{\rm pr}_{K_{1},K_{0}^{\prime},\ast}(z_{1}^{\underline{k}})

\displaystyle=({\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{p}}\circ\eta_{\mathfrak{p}})_{*}\left({\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{G,*}}\circ\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S},*}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}})-\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S},*}\circ{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S},\emptyset}^{H,*}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}})\right)

\displaystyle=T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot\left(\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S},*}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}})\right)-({\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{p}}\circ\eta_{\mathfrak{p}})_{*}\left(\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S},*}\circ{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S},\emptyset}^{H,*}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}})\right).

We now examine the last term of the right hand side. Since
\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}
defines an element of
H({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
and
u_{\mathfrak{S}}K_{0}(\mathfrak{S})u_{\mathfrak{S}}^{-1}\cap H
has
\mathfrak{p}
-component equal to
\Gamma_{H,0}(\pi_{\mathfrak{p}})
, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{Y_{H}(u_{\mathfrak{S}}K_{0}^{\prime}u_{\mathfrak{S}}^{-1}\cap H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{H}\circ\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S}}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{0}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{p}}\circ\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}
\textstyle{Y_{H}(u_{\mathfrak{S}}K_{0}(\mathfrak{S})u_{\mathfrak{S}}^{-1}\cap H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S}}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{S})).}

We thus have

({\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{p}}\circ\eta_{\mathfrak{p}})_{*}\circ\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S},*}=\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S},*}\circ({\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{H}\circ\eta_{\mathfrak{p}})_{*}.

This implies that

\displaystyle({\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{p}}\circ\eta_{\mathfrak{p}})_{*}\left(\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S},*}\circ{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S},\emptyset}^{H,*}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}})\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S},*}\circ({\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{H}\circ\eta_{\mathfrak{p}})_{*}\circ{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S},\emptyset}^{H,*}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}}).

The Hecke correspondence
({\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{H}\circ\eta_{\mathfrak{p}})_{*}\circ{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S},\emptyset}^{H,*}
commutes with
\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}
and acts on

\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}}\in H^{0}(Y_{H}(u_{\mathfrak{S}}K_{0}(\mathfrak{S})u_{\mathfrak{S}}^{-1}\cap H),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})

by
q_{\mathfrak{p}}\cdot p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}k_{\sigma}}
, with
q_{\mathfrak{p}}
being the degree of
{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{H}
. Therefore we get

\displaystyle({\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{p}}\circ\eta_{\mathfrak{p}})_{*}\circ{\rm pr}_{K_{1},K_{0}^{\prime},\ast}(z_{1}^{\underline{k}})

\displaystyle=\left(T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}-q_{\mathfrak{p}}\cdot p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}k_{\sigma}}\right)\cdot\left(\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S},*}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}})\right).

We now reiterate the same proof for each prime in
\Upsilon_{F,p}
. Let
{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}\in\mathfrak{S}
and denote
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}:=\mathfrak{S}\smallsetminus\{{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}\}
. By Proposition
4.23
, we have the Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{Y_{H}(u_{\mathfrak{S^{\prime}}}K_{0}(\mathfrak{S})u_{\mathfrak{S^{\prime}}}^{-1}\cap H)\sqcup Y_{H}(u_{\mathfrak{S}}K_{0}(\mathfrak{S})u_{\mathfrak{S}}^{-1}\cap H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{({\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime},\emptyset}^{H},{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime},{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}}^{H})}
\scriptstyle{(\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}},\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S}})}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{S}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}}^{G}}
\textstyle{Y_{H}(u_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}}K_{0}(\mathfrak{S}^{\prime})u_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}}^{-1}\cap H)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{S}^{\prime})).}

This Cartesian diagram allows us to write

\displaystyle\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S},*}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}})={\pi_{{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}}^{G,*}}\circ\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime},*}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}})-\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime},*}\circ{\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime},\emptyset}^{H,*}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}}),

where we have again denoted
\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}}:=\mathbf{1}_{u_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}}K_{0}(\mathfrak{S}^{\prime})u_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}}^{-1}\cap H}
. Exactly as above, after applying the normalized trace of

Y_{G}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{S}))\xrightarrow{{\rm pr}_{{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}}\circ\eta_{{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}}}Y_{G}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{S}^{\prime})),

we get

\displaystyle({\rm pr}_{{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}}\circ\eta_{{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}})_{*}\circ\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S},*}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}})

\displaystyle=\left(T_{\eta_{{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}}}-q_{{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}}\cdot p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}}}k_{\sigma}}\right)\cdot\left(\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime},*}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}})\right).

As the trace
({\rm pr}_{{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}}\circ\eta_{{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}})_{*}
commutes with the correspondence
\left(T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}-q_{\mathfrak{p}}\cdot p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}k_{\sigma}}\right)
, we thus have

\displaystyle({\rm pr}_{{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}}\circ\eta_{{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}})_{*}\circ({\rm pr}_{\mathfrak{p}}\circ\eta_{\mathfrak{p}})_{*}\circ{\rm pr}_{K_{1},K_{0}^{\prime},\ast}(z_{1}^{\underline{k}})

\displaystyle=\left(T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}-q_{\mathfrak{p}}\cdot p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}k_{\sigma}}\right)\left(T_{\eta_{{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}}}-q_{{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}}\cdot p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}}}k_{\sigma}}\right)\cdot\left(\tilde{\iota}_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime},*}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}})\right).

We now reiterate the process for each remaining prime in
\Upsilon_{F,p}
to get

\eta_{K_{0}^{\prime},K_{0},\ast}\circ{\rm pr}_{K_{1},K_{0}^{\prime},\ast}(z_{1}^{\underline{k}})=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}\left(T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}-q_{\mathfrak{p}}\cdot p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}k_{\sigma}}\right)\cdot\left(\iota_{0,*}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{K_{0}\cap H})\right),

which reads as

{\rm Tr}_{[\eta]_{1,0}}(z_{1}^{\underline{k}})=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}\left(T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}-q_{\mathfrak{p}}\cdot p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}k_{\sigma}}\right)\cdot z_{0}^{\underline{k}}.

∎

Recall that
\mathscr{Z}^{\underline{k}}_{1}:=\eta_{\ast}z^{\underline{k}}_{1}
, with
\eta_{\ast}
the normalized trace associated with multiplication by
\eta
from
Y_{G}(K_{1})
to
Y_{G}(V_{1})
. Proposition
5.5
translates into the following :

Theorem 5.6
.

Let
\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Sigma_{E}}
be such that
k_{\sigma}=k_{\sigma^{\prime}}
for every
\sigma\in\Phi
. We have

{\rm pr}_{1,0,\ast}(\mathscr{Z}_{1}^{\underline{k}})=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}\left(T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}-q_{\mathfrak{p}}\cdot p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}k_{\sigma}}\right)\cdot\mathscr{Z}_{0}^{\underline{k}}\in H^{2d}(Y_{G}(V_{0}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}).

Proof.

It follows from Proposition
5.5
and the commutative diagram

\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\scriptstyle{[\eta]_{1,0}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(V_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm pr}_{1,0}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(V_{0}).}

∎

Recall that we have the moment map

\mathrm{mom}^{\underline{k}}_{1}:H^{i}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})\rightarrow H^{i}(Y_{G}(V_{1}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}).

Denote by
\mathrm{mom}^{\underline{k}}_{0}
the composition of
\mathrm{mom}^{\underline{k}}_{1}
with
{\rm pr}_{1,0,\ast}
. Then we have the following :

Corollary 5.7
.

Let
\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Sigma_{E}}
be such that
k_{\sigma}=k_{\sigma^{\prime}}
for every
\sigma\in\Phi
. Then,

\mathrm{mom}^{\underline{k}}_{0}\,\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}\left(1-q_{\mathfrak{p}}\cdot p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}k_{\sigma}}\,T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{-1}\right)\cdot e^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}\mathscr{Z}_{0}^{\underline{k}}.

Proof.

It follows from Theorem
5.4
and Theorem
5.6
.
∎

Remark 5.8
.

Analogously, we could prove an explicit relation between
\mathrm{mom}^{\underline{k}}_{\boldsymbol{r}}\,\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}
and
e^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}\mathscr{Z}_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\underline{k}}
, where
r_{\mathfrak{p}}
is zero for
\mathfrak{p}
in a proper subset
\mathfrak{S}
of
\Upsilon_{F,p}
. In this case, the formula would read as

\mathrm{mom}^{\underline{k}}_{\boldsymbol{r}}\,\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{S}}\left(1-q_{\mathfrak{p}}\cdot p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}k_{\sigma}}\,T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{-1}\right)\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}\smallsetminus\mathfrak{S}}T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{-r_{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot e^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}\mathscr{Z}_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\underline{k}}.

We leave the details to the interested reader.

5.4.
Twisting the universal cycles

We make precise the choice of the tame level and show how we can further twist the big Hirzebruch–Zagier cycles by certain characters of conductor a power of
p
.

Let
\mathfrak{c}
be an ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{E}
assumed to be coprime with
p
in this section, and let
\mathfrak{c}_{F}
be its intersection with
\mathcal{O}_{F}
. Let
\chi_{F}
be a finite order Hecke character for
F
of conductor
\mathfrak{c}_{F}
and let
\chi=\chi_{F}\circ{\rm N}_{E/F}
, which we view as a character of
K_{0}(\mathfrak{c})
. Consider also a unitary Hecke character
\theta
of
E
of conductor
\mathfrak{b}
, coprime with
p
, and whose restriction to
\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{F}
is
\chi_{F}\eta_{E/F}
.
Set
K^{(p)}=K_{0}(\mathfrak{c})
, with
\mathfrak{c}
stable under
\tau\in{\rm Gal}(E/F)
. As the Hecke correspondences used in Definitions
3.10
and
3.11
commute with
T_{\eta}
and the action of
u
, we can analogously define the twisted cycles
\mathscr{Z}_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\underline{k},\chi}(\mathfrak{c}),\mathscr{Z}_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\underline{k}}(\mathfrak{c})_{\theta}\in H^{2d}(Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}}),\mathscr{V}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\underline{k},0}(\chi))
, up to possibly enlarging the coefficient ring
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}
, where (see Definition
4.9
)

V_{\boldsymbol{r}}=K_{0}(\mathfrak{c})\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}V_{\mathfrak{p},r_{\mathfrak{p}}},

as well as
\mathscr{Z}_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\underline{k},\chi^{-1}\theta^{2}}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})\in H^{2d}(Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}}),\mathscr{V}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\underline{k},0}(\chi^{-1}\theta^{2}))
where the tame component of
V_{\boldsymbol{r}}
is
K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})
.
By Theorem
5.4
, these cycles can still be assembled into a
p
-adic family of cycles and denote by
\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}^{\chi}
and
\mathscr{Z}_{\infty,\theta}
the corresponding twisted big Hirzebruch–Zagier cycles in

e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi))\simeq e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}(Y_{G}(K_{0}(p^{2}\mathfrak{c})),\Lambda_{G/H}(\chi)),

and similarly for
\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}^{\chi^{-1}\theta^{2}}
. Note that we could further twist these big Hirzebruch–Zagier cycles by finite order characters of
T_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})/\overline{\mathcal{E}}L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
as the following shows.

Let
\chi^{\prime}
be any finite order character of conductor
p^{r}
which factors through
T_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{r})/\overline{\mathcal{E}}L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{r})
, and denote also by
\chi^{\prime}
the induced morphism of
\Lambda_{G/H}
. Note that the decomposition

\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}[T_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{r})/{\mathcal{E}}(p^{r})L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{r})][1/p]\cong\prod_{\chi^{\prime}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}[1/p],

where
\chi^{\prime}
ranges through such
L_{\mathfrak{m}}
-valued characters of conductor
p^{r}
is given by the idempotent elements

e_{\chi^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{|T_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{r})/{\mathcal{E}}(p^{r})L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{r})|}\sum_{\gamma\in T_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{r})/{\mathcal{E}}(p^{r})L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{r})}{\chi^{\prime}}^{-1}(\gamma)\gamma^{*}.

For any
\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Sigma_{E}}
such that
k_{\sigma}=k_{\sigma^{\prime}}
for every
\sigma\in\Phi
, the idempotent
e_{\chi^{\prime}}
induces the map
\mathrm{mom}_{r,\chi^{\prime}}^{\underline{k}}
in cohomology, which is defined as the composition

\displaystyle e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{}e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\chi))

\displaystyle\to e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}(Y_{G}(K_{0}(p^{r+1}\mathfrak{c})),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{L_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\chi{\chi^{\prime}}^{-1})),

where the first map is
\mathrm{mom}_{r}^{\underline{k}}
and the second one is given by the composition

\frac{1}{|T_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{r})/{\mathcal{E}}(p^{r})L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{r})|}\sum_{\gamma\in T_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{r})/{\mathcal{E}}(p^{r})L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{r})}{\chi^{\prime}}^{-1}(\gamma){\rm pr}^{\gamma V_{r}\gamma^{-1}}_{K_{0}(p^{r+1}\mathfrak{c}),\star}\circ\gamma^{-1}_{*}.

Here, we see
\chi^{\prime}
as a character of
K_{0}(p^{r+1}\mathfrak{c})
by setting

\chi^{\prime}_{v}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\begin{cases}\chi^{\prime}_{v}(\tau(a)^{-1}d)&\text{ if }\mathfrak{p}_{v}\mid\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}\\
1&\text{ otherwise,}\end{cases}

hence we can define the local system
\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{L_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\chi{\chi^{\prime}}^{-1})
over
Y_{G}(K_{0}(p^{r+1}\mathfrak{c}))
as in
[
17
, Proposition 6.3]
. More generally, if
\chi^{\prime}
is a character of conductor
p^{\boldsymbol{r}}
, for
\boldsymbol{r}=(r_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{p}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{|\Upsilon_{F,p}|}
, one can define a map

\mathrm{mom}_{\boldsymbol{r},\chi^{\prime}}^{\underline{k}}:e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi))\to e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}(Y_{G}(K_{0}(p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}\mathfrak{c})),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{L_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\chi{\chi^{\prime}}^{-1})),

by using the idempotent

e_{\chi^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{|K_{0}(p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}\mathfrak{c})/\mathcal{O}_{E}^{*,+}V_{\boldsymbol{r}}|}\sum_{\gamma\in K_{0}(p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}\mathfrak{c})/\mathcal{O}_{E}^{*,+}V_{\boldsymbol{r}}}{\chi^{\prime}}^{-1}(\gamma){\rm pr}^{\gamma V_{\boldsymbol{r}}\gamma^{-1}}_{K_{0}(p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}\mathfrak{c}),\star}\circ\gamma^{-1}_{*}.

Definition 5.9
.

For a character
\chi^{\prime}
of conductor
p^{\boldsymbol{r}}
, we let

\mathscr{Z}_{\boldsymbol{r},\chi^{\prime}}^{\underline{k},\chi}:=\mathrm{mom}_{\boldsymbol{r},\chi^{\prime}}^{\underline{k}}\,\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}^{\chi}\in e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}(Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}}),\mathscr{V}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\underline{k},0}(\chi{\chi^{\prime}}^{-1})).

These twisted cycles, which won’t be used in the following, can be seen as a
p
-adic counterpart of Getz–Goresky’s cycles of Definition
3.11
.

6.
Hida theory and base change

Consider a weight
\kappa=(\underline{k},m)\in({\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0})^{\Sigma_{E}}\times{\mathbb{Z}}
and let
L
be a large enough number field to contain the Galois closure of
E
. As in the previous section, we let
\mathfrak{m}
be a prime of
L
above
p
and take the
\mathfrak{m}
-adic completion
L_{\mathfrak{m}}
, with ring of integers
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}
.
For every prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}
of
F
dividing
p
we let
\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}
be the subset of
\Sigma_{F}
of (
p
-adic) places
\sigma
sending
\mathfrak{p}
to the maximal ideal of the valuation ring of
F_{\mathfrak{p}}
. We introduce two Hecke operators on
S_{\kappa}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}),\chi)
, for any ideal
\mathfrak{c}
in
\mathcal{O}_{E}
with
\mathfrak{p}\mid\mathfrak{c}
: the usual Hecke operator
U_{\mathfrak{p}}
defined using the double coset operator
\left[K_{0}(\mathfrak{c})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)K_{0}(\mathfrak{c})\right]
and the normalized
U_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}:={p}^{{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}\frac{k_{\sigma}-m}{2}}}U_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
8
8

8

The term nearly ordinary goes back to Hida’s first works on families for Hilbert modular forms. It is to stress that, except in the case of parallel weight Hilbert modular forms, the right notion of ordinarity is not slope-zero but slope
p^{-{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{F}}\frac{k_{\sigma}-m}{2}}}
. With the development of Hida theory in more general settings
[
26
,
7
]
, it is more customary to simply say ordinary, and implicitly referring to an optimally normalized
U_{p}
operator. As we work with Hilbert modular forms, we prefer to use Hida’s original terminology. See
[
23
, §2]
.

Lemma 6.1
.

The map

S_{\kappa}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}),\chi)\to H^{2d}(Y_{G}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c})),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},{-m}}(\chi^{-1}_{0})_{\mathbb{C}}),\,f\mapsto[\omega_{J}(f^{-\iota})],

is Hecke-equivariant in the sense that

\omega_{J}((f_{|K_{0}(\mathfrak{c})xK_{0}(\mathfrak{c})})^{-\iota})=T^{\text{naive}}_{x^{\iota}}\cdot\omega_{J}(f^{-\iota}),

where we denoted
T^{\text{naive}}_{x^{\iota}}=\pi_{2,\ast}\circ x^{\iota}_{\ast}\circ\pi_{1}^{\ast}
the correspondence for

\textstyle{Y_{G}(x^{\iota}K_{0}(\mathfrak{c})x^{-\iota}\cap K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\cdot x^{\iota}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c})\cap x^{-\iota}K_{0}(\mathfrak{c})x^{\iota})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}))}
\textstyle{Y_{G}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c})),}

with
\pi_{i}
’s the natural degeneracy maps and
x^{\iota}=\mathrm{det}(x)x^{-1}
.

Proof.

The Hecke equivariance follows from
[
17
, Proposition 7.13]
and the Remark at p.60 of
loc.cit.
.
∎

Twist the map of Lemma
6.1
with the Atkin–Lehner involution to get

(26)

\displaystyle S_{\kappa}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}),\chi)\to H^{2d}(Y_{G}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c})),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},{-m}}(\chi_{0})_{\mathbb{C}}),\,f\mapsto[\omega_{J}(W_{\mathfrak{c}}^{*}f^{-\iota})].

Then, the Hecke correspondence
T_{\eta}
introduced in Definition
4.13
and
U_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}
are related as follows.

Proposition 6.2
.

Under the map (
26
), if
\mathfrak{p}\mid\mathfrak{c}
, the normalized correspondence

T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}=p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}\frac{k_{\sigma}-m}{2}}T^{\text{naive}}_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}},

with
\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, corresponds to
U_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}
on
S_{\kappa}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}),\chi)
, and
T_{\eta}=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}}U_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}
.

Proof.

By the commutative diagram (
3.1.4
), it is enough to check how the adjoint Hecke correspondence

T^{\text{naive},*}_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}=W_{\mathfrak{c},*}\circ T^{\text{naive}}_{{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}}\circ W_{\mathfrak{c}}^{*}

acts on
\omega_{J}(f^{-\iota})
. Note that
T^{\text{naive},*}_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}
is associated to the element
{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
and by Lemma
6.1
it acts on
\omega_{J}(f^{-\iota})
as
U_{\mathfrak{p}}
. The result then follows from comparing the two normalization factors.
∎

In view of Proposition
6.2
we define an idempotent
e^{\rm n.ord}:=\lim_{n}\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}{{U_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}}}^{n!}
, that we denote as the nearly-ordinary operator of Lemma
4.31
.
Hida proved that it is well-defined on

H^{\ast}(Y_{G}(K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\alpha})),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},m}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})

for all
\mathfrak{c}
and
{\alpha}\geq 0
.
As in
[
13
, §3.2]
, we let
\mathcal{H}^{G}_{11}(\underline{k},{m})
be

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\left(\varinjlim_{\alpha\geq 1}e^{\rm n.ord}H^{\star}_{c}(Y_{G}(K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\alpha})),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},{m}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}L_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}),L_{\mathfrak{m}}/\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}\right).

Hida’s main result
[
21
, Theorem 2.3]
is that the space

\mathcal{H}^{G}_{11}(\underline{k},{m})

is naturally a finite and torsion-free algebra over the Iwasawa algebra

\Lambda_{G}:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}\llbracket\varprojlim_{\alpha,\beta\geq 1}\mathbb{A}_{E,f}^{\times}/E^{\times}(\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{E,f}\cap K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\alpha}))\times(\mathcal{O}_{E}/p^{\beta})^{\times}\rrbracket

By
[
21
, Lemma 2.1]
, the action of
(z,u)\in\varprojlim_{\alpha,\beta\geq 1}\mathbb{A}_{E,f}^{\times}/E^{\times}(\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{E,f}\cap K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\alpha}))\times(\mathcal{O}_{E}/p^{\beta})^{\times}
corresponds to the action of the matrix
{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}uz&0\\
0&z\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}
, where with a slight abuse of notation
u
denotes a lift to
\prod_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{E,\mathfrak{p}}
(hence
u
has non-trivial component only at
p
).

Remark 6.3
.

(1)

If
p
does not divide the cardinality of
\mathbb{A}_{E,f}^{\times}/E^{\times}(\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{E,f}\cap K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\alpha}))
then this group (and so the associated Iwasawa algebra) splits canonically as a torsion-part and a non-torsion part.

(2)

Note that the variable
z
is essentially the cyclotomic variable (assuming Leopoldt conjecture for
E
) and interpolates the weight
m
, and
u
in
\prod_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{E,\mathfrak{p}}
interpolates the weight
\underline{k}
.

Hida also shows that
\mathcal{H}^{G}_{11}(\underline{k},{m})
is independent of the weight, in the sense that we have canonical isomorphisms (see
[
13
, Theorem 3.1]
)

\mathcal{H}^{G}_{11}(\underline{k},{m})\cong\mathcal{H}^{G}_{11}(\underline{0},{0})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}v_{\underline{k}}^{\rm hw},

with
v_{\underline{k}}^{\rm hw}
the highest weight vector in
V^{(\underline{k},{m})}
. For ease of notation, we shall often drop the dependence on the weight from the notation, thus writing only
\mathcal{H}^{G}_{11}
.

We refer to
\mathcal{H}^{G}_{11}(\underline{k},{m})
as the cuspidal
p
-adic ordinary Hecke algebra as it is isomorphic to the
p
-adic Hecke algebra constructed using cuspidal Hilbert modular forms as in
[
23
, §3]
: see the proofs of Theorem 3.2 and 3.3 in
[
20
, §11]
), which use the duality between the space of modular forms and the Hecke algebra, neatly stated in
[
27
, Thm 2.28]
.

We define
\mathcal{H}^{H}_{11}
and
\Lambda_{H}
similarly. the module
\mathcal{H}^{H}_{11}
is of finite type over
\Lambda_{H}
and interpolates systems of Hecke eigenvalues for nearly-ordinary Hilbert modular forms for
F
.

Definition 6.4
.

A Hida family
\mathbf{f}
for
F
, resp. for
E
, is an irreducible component of
\mathcal{H}^{H}_{11}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}L_{\mathfrak{m}}
, resp.
\mathcal{H}^{G}_{11}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}L_{\mathfrak{m}}
. We shall denote by
\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{f}}
the fraction field of the coefficient of
\mathbf{f}
.

We invert
p
as in what follows we will need to avoid congruences between base change forms and non-base change forms.

6.1.
Base change families

Recall that, for a given weight
\kappa=(\underline{k},m)
for
F
, we let
\hat{\kappa}=(\hat{\underline{k}},m)
be the weight for
E
for which
\hat{k}_{\sigma}=\hat{k}_{\sigma^{\prime}}
for every
\sigma\in\Phi
.
We have the following proposition, see
[
17
, §8.2]
.

Proposition 6.5
.

There is a base change map

\mathrm{BC}:\mathcal{H}^{G}_{11}(\hat{\underline{k}},{m})\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{H}_{11}(\underline{k},{m})

defined as follows: for every prime ideal
\mathfrak{q}\subset\mathcal{O}_{E}
, with
\mathfrak{q}
coprime to
\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathfrak{d}_{E/F}\mathcal{O}_{E}
, which is above
\mathfrak{r}
in
\mathcal{O}_{F}
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{BC}(T_{\mathfrak{q}})

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}T_{\mathfrak{r}}&\text{ if }\mathfrak{r}\text{ splits},\mathfrak{r}=\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{q}^{\prime},\\
T_{\mathfrak{r}^{2}}-N_{F/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{r})S_{\mathfrak{r}}&\mbox{ if }\mathfrak{r}\mbox{ inert},\mathfrak{r}=\mathfrak{q},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathrm{BC}(S_{\mathfrak{q}})

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}S_{\mathfrak{r}}&\text{ if }\mathfrak{r}\text{ splits},\mathfrak{r}=\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{q}^{\prime},\\
S_{\mathfrak{r}^{2}}&\mbox{ if }\mathfrak{r}\mbox{ inert},\mathfrak{r}=\mathfrak{q}.\end{cases}

Over
\mathbb{C}
, there is a natural section of
\mathrm{BC}
(
cf
.
[
17
, Lemma 8.1]
).

Definition 6.6
.

Let
f_{E}
be a primitive normalized Hilbert modular form of weight
(\hat{\underline{k}},m)
. We say that
f_{E}
is the base change of a (primitive) Hilbert modular form
f
for
F
if the corresponding map of the Hecke algebra
\mathcal{H}^{G}_{11}(\hat{\underline{k}},{m})
factors through
\mathrm{BC}
.

This is compatible with the characterization given in Proposition
3.7
: if
f_{E}
is the base change of
f
then the corresponding cuspidal automorphic representations are related by

\widehat{\pi(f)}=\pi(f_{E}).

Note that the conductor of
f
is not necessarily the conductor of
f_{E}
intersected with
\mathcal{O}_{F}
(
cf
.
[
17
, §E.6]
). Also note that
\mathrm{BC}
maps nearly ordinary forms to nearly ordinary forms.

We define base change Hida families. Using the map of Proposition
6.5
, we could define a Hida family for
E
to be a base change family from
F
if it factors through
\mathrm{BC}
. We however refine this definition to better suit the construction of the adjoint
p
-adic
L
-function given in §
7.3
. We first decompose

\mathbb{A}_{E,f}^{\times}/E^{\times}(\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{E,f}\cap K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\alpha}))={\mathbb{A}_{E,f}^{(p)}}^{\times}/E^{\times}(\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{E,f}\cap K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}))\times(E\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}/E^{\times}((E\otimes\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\cap K_{11}(p^{\alpha}))

and similarly do it for
F
.

Definition 6.7
.

Let
\chi_{0}
be a character of
\mathbb{A}_{E,f}^{\times}/E^{\times}(\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{E,f}\cap K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}))
with values in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}
. The nearly-ordinary Hecke-algebra of type
\Phi
and tame character
\chi_{0}
is

\mathcal{H}^{G/H}_{11}:=\mathcal{H}^{G}_{11}\otimes_{\Lambda_{G}}\Lambda_{G/H}

where the map

\Lambda_{G}\rightarrow\Lambda_{G/H}

is induced by the map

\varprojlim_{\alpha,\beta\geq 1}\mathbb{A}_{E,f}^{\times}/E^{\times}(\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{E,f}\cap K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\alpha}))\times(\mathcal{O}_{E}/p^{\beta})^{\times}\rightarrow T_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})/\overline{\mathcal{E}}L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})

that sends
(1,u)
to the image of the matrix
{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}u&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}
in
T_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})/\overline{\mathcal{E}}L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
and
(z,1)
, after writing
z=z^{(p)}z_{p}
, to
\chi_{0}(z^{(p)}){{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}z_{p}&\\
&z_{p}\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\in\Lambda_{G/H}
, where the matrix is modulo
\overline{\mathcal{E}}L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
.

Our goal is to define base change families starting from irreducible components of
\mathcal{H}^{G/H}_{11}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}L_{\mathfrak{m}}
. Before we do so, notice that if
f
is a cuspidal Hilbert modular form for
F
of level
K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}_{F}p^{\alpha})
with trivial Nebentypus at
p
, then the base-change
f_{E}
has weights constrained to have
k_{\sigma}=k_{\sigma^{\prime}}
and trivial Nebentypus at
p
. Moreover, its adjoint character (see Definition
3.3
) factors through
{\rm N}_{E/F}
and so has
p
-component trivial on
\overline{\mathcal{E}}L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
. Motivated by this, we give the following :

Definition 6.8
.

Let
\Lambda_{H/Z_{H}}:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}\llbracket T_{H}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})/Z_{H}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\rrbracket

and let
\chi_{F,0}
be a character of
\mathbb{A}_{F,f}^{\times}/F^{\times}(\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{F,f}\cap K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}_{F}))
with values in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}
. Define

\mathcal{H}^{H/Z_{H}}_{11}:=\mathcal{H}^{H}_{11}\otimes_{\Lambda_{H}}\Lambda_{H/Z_{H}}

where the map
\Lambda_{H}\rightarrow\Lambda_{H/Z_{H}}

is induced by the map

\varprojlim_{\alpha,\beta\geq 1}\mathbb{A}_{F,f}^{\times}/F^{\times}(\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{F,f}\cap K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}_{F}p^{\alpha}))\times(\mathcal{O}_{F}/p^{\beta})^{\times}\rightarrow T_{H}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})/Z_{H}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})

that sends
(1,u)
to the image of the matrix
{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}u&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}
in
T_{H}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})/Z_{H}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
and
(z,1)
, with
z=z^{(p)}z_{p}
, to
\chi_{F,0}(z^{(p)})
.

As
\mathrm{BC}
induces a map
\mathcal{H}^{G/H}_{11}\to\mathcal{H}^{H/Z_{H}}_{11}
, we can define base change families as follows.

Definition 6.9
.

We say that a family, (
i.e.
an irreducible component of
\mathcal{H}^{G/H}_{11}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}L_{\mathfrak{m}}
) is a base change family (from
F
) if it factors through
\mathcal{H}^{H/Z_{H}}_{11}
via the map
\mathrm{BC}
of Proposition
6.5
, for two compatible levels.

Remark 6.10
.

(1)

We note that the tame character of a base change family necessarily factors through
{\rm N}_{E/F}
.

(2)

As we are fixing the component outside
p
of the Nebentypus of the family and we later force
m=0
, all the Hilbert modular forms for
F
coming from our families will have trivial central character/Nebentypus at
p
and only an adjoint character (in the sense of Definition
3.3
). There is no loss of generality for the range of applications in this manuscript as the adjoint
L
-function is invariant by central twists.

Proposition 6.11
.

There is an idempotent
\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{BC}}
in
\mathcal{H}^{G/H}_{11}\otimes\mathrm{Frac}(\Lambda_{G/H})
such that

\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{BC}}\mathcal{H}^{G/H}_{11}

consists exactly only of primitive base change families.

Proof.

As the Hecke operators are commutative and the nearly-ordinary Hecke algebra is of finite type, then

\mathcal{H}^{G/H}_{11}\otimes\mathrm{Frac}(\Lambda_{G/H})

is semi-local. Then
\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{BC}}
is the idempotent associated with the sum of the idempotent of base change families which are primitive.
∎

6.2.
The
p
-stabilisation and forms of level
V_{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}

We write down explicitly the
p
-stabilisation of a nearly-ordinary cuspidal Hilbert modular form (for
F
)
f\in S_{\kappa}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}),\chi,\chi^{\prime})
. Analogous definitions apply to forms for
E
. We refer the reader to
[
23
, Lemma 5.3]
for details.

Suppose
f
is nearly ordinary at
\mathfrak{p}
,
i.e.

U_{\mathfrak{p}}f=a(\mathfrak{p},f)f
and
\left|a(\mathfrak{p},f){p}^{{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}\frac{k_{\sigma}-m}{2}}}\right|_{p}=1
. There are three possible cases :

(St)

If the corresponding automorphic representation is special at
\mathfrak{p}
, then the level
\mathfrak{c}
must be divisible exactly by
\mathfrak{p}
.

(RamPS)

If
\mathfrak{p}
divides the conductor of
\chi
or
\chi^{\prime}
, then
\mathfrak{c}
must be divisible by
\mathfrak{p}
.

(UnrPS)

If none of the above is true, then the two Satake parameters
{\alpha_{1,\mathfrak{p}}},{\alpha_{2,\mathfrak{p}}}
are both non-zero.

In case (UnrPS), only one of these, say
{\alpha_{1,\mathfrak{p}}}
, is such that
\left|{\alpha_{1,\mathfrak{p}}}\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{1/2}{p}^{{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}\frac{k_{\sigma}-m}{2}}}\right|_{p}=1
.
We shall write
{\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}}={\alpha_{1,\mathfrak{p}}}
,
{\alpha^{\circ}_{\mathfrak{p}}}={\alpha_{1,\mathfrak{p}}}{p}^{{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}\frac{k_{\sigma}-m}{2}}}
, and
\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}={\alpha_{2,\mathfrak{p}}}
.

We define the
\mathfrak{p}
-stabilisation
f_{{\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}}}
of
f
by

f_{{\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}}}:=f-\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{1/2}{\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}}f|[\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}]

for
[\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}]
the operator of
[
23
, §7.B]
, given by the slash action of the matrix
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}}&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. Note that we have

a(\mathfrak{p},f_{{\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}}})={\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{1/2}.

In general, we shall write
f_{\alpha}
for the composite of all the
\mathfrak{p}
-stabilisation of
f
, for
\mathfrak{p}
for which
f
is not primitive at
\mathfrak{p}
.

We conclude with a lemma comparing forms (for
E
) of level
V_{\boldsymbol{r}}
with forms of level
K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\boldsymbol{r}})
, with
\mathfrak{c}
coprime with
p\mathcal{O}_{E}
.
Here, for
\boldsymbol{r}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{|\Upsilon_{F,p}|}
, we let
p^{\boldsymbol{r}}=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}\prod_{\mathfrak{P}|\mathfrak{p}}\mathfrak{P}^{r_{\mathfrak{p}}}
and

\displaystyle V_{\boldsymbol{r}}

\displaystyle=K_{11}(\mathfrak{c})\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}V_{\mathfrak{p},r_{\mathfrak{p}}}.

Firstly, observe that, if
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
are characters of
p
-part conductor
p^{{\boldsymbol{r}}}
such that at each prime
\mathfrak{P}\mid p^{\boldsymbol{r}}

(27)

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathfrak{P}}(d)\chi^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{P}}(a^{-1}d)=1,\text{ for }d\equiv\tau(a)\text{ mod }\mathfrak{P}^{r_{\mathfrak{p}}},

then any form
f\in S_{\kappa}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}),\chi,\chi^{\prime})
is invariant by
V_{\boldsymbol{r}}
.

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
f
be a modular form in
S_{\kappa}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}p^{{\boldsymbol{r}}}),\chi,\chi^{\prime})
for two characters
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
with
p
-part of conductor
p^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1}}
and
p^{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{2}}
respectively. Suppose
{\boldsymbol{r}}=\max\left\{{\boldsymbol{r}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{r}}_{2}\right\}
. Then the injection
f\mapsto f

S_{\kappa}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}p^{{\boldsymbol{r}}}),\chi,\chi^{\prime})\hookrightarrow S_{\kappa}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}),\chi,\chi^{\prime})

is an isomorphism on the ordinary parts.

Proof.

By the control theorem of Hida
[
23
, Corollary 3.3]
every ordinary form of
S_{\kappa}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}),\chi,\chi^{\prime})
is in
S_{\kappa}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}p^{{\boldsymbol{r}}}),\chi,\chi^{\prime})
, as
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
are both characters modulo
p^{{\boldsymbol{r}}}
. So the natural injection is an isomorphism.
∎

Remark 6.13
.

Let
\mathfrak{c}_{F}
be the intersection of
\mathfrak{c}
with
\mathcal{O}_{F}
. Consider a nearly ordinary form (for
F
)
f\in\mathcal{S}_{(\underline{k},0)}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}_{F}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}),\chi_{F},\chi_{F}^{\prime})
such that, at each
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}
,
\chi_{F}
has trivial
\mathfrak{p}
-component and the
\mathfrak{p}
-part of
\chi_{F}^{\prime}
has conductor exactly
\mathfrak{p}^{r_{\mathfrak{p}}}
. Let
f_{E}\in\mathcal{S}_{(\hat{\underline{k}},0)}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}),\chi,\chi^{\prime})
be the base change of
f
. Then
f_{E}
has Nebentypus
\chi=\chi_{F}\circ{\rm N}_{E/F}
with trivial component at every prime above
p
. Moreover, by
[
17
, Proposition E.9]
, it has adjoint character
\chi^{\prime}=\chi_{F}^{\prime}\circ{\rm N}_{E/F}
, whose
\mathfrak{P}
-part has conductor
\mathfrak{P}^{r_{\mathfrak{p}}}
if
\mathfrak{P}|\mathfrak{p}
, for every
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}
. In particular, (
27
) is satisfied and
f_{E}
is invariant by
V_{\boldsymbol{r}}
.

6.3.
The idempotent for a Hida family with type
J

Let
\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{f}}
be the idempotent corresponding to the Hida family
\mathbf{f}
of Hilbert modular forms for
E
. It acts on
\varinjlim_{\alpha}H^{2d}_{c}(Y_{G}(K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\alpha})),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},{m}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})
, and over this space there is an action of the Hecke algebra at infinity, generated by the operators
[K_{\infty}^{+}g_{\infty}K_{\infty}^{+}]
, for
g_{\infty}\in\left\{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\pm 1&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}\right\}^{\Sigma_{E}}
. As
p
is odd, the action of this operator can be diagonalised: for an
F
-type
J
, we let
\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}
be the idempotent corresponding to the eigenspace for
\varepsilon
, where the only requirement on
\varepsilon
is that, for every
\sigma\in J
,

\varepsilon({\big(\begin{smallmatrix}-1&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)})_{\sigma}\varepsilon({\big(\begin{smallmatrix}-1&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)})_{\sigma^{\prime}}=-1.

We could take, for example
\varepsilon
such that

\varepsilon({\big(\begin{smallmatrix}-1&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)})_{\sigma}=1
if
\sigma\in J
and
-1
otherwise. We finally define
\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}}
to be

\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}}:=\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{f}}.

Recall we fixed an isomorphism
i_{p}:{\mathbb{C}}\simeq\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{p}
. Suppose that the Hida family is made of primitive forms (see
[
23
, Theorem 3.4]
) and let
f
be a classical specialisation of the family corresponding to a morphism

P:\mathcal{H}^{G}_{11}\rightarrow L_{\mathfrak{m}}.

Then the space
\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}}\varinjlim_{\alpha}H^{2d}_{c}(Y_{G}(K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\alpha})),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},{m}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})\otimes_{P}L_{\mathfrak{m}}
is one-dimensional over
L_{\mathfrak{m}}
, see the bottom of p. 38 in
[
25
]
.
Moreover by
loc. cit.
the surjection
H^{2d}_{c}(Y_{G}(K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\alpha})),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},{m}}_{{\mathbb{C}}})\twoheadrightarrow H^{2d}_{\rm cusp}(Y_{G}(K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\alpha})),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},{m}}_{{\mathbb{C}}})
admits a canonical section. Hence, if we start with a nearly ordinary form
f\in S_{\kappa}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\alpha}),\chi)
, the class
\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}\omega_{J}(W^{*}_{\mathfrak{c}p^{\alpha}}f^{-\iota})
belongs to
\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}}\varinjlim_{\alpha}H^{2d}_{c}(Y_{G}(K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\alpha})),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},{m}}_{L_{\mathfrak{m}}})\otimes_{\iota_{p}^{-1}}{\mathbb{C}}
and it is a generator of this one dimensional space over
\mathbb{C}
. We note that the presence of the Atkin–Lehner involution is motivated by the fact that, in view of Proposition
6.2
, the map of Lemma
6.1
becomes
U_{p}
-equivariant after twisting by the Atkin–Lehner involution.
If
\omega^{\varepsilon}(f)^{\mathrm{alg}}
is a generator of
\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}}H^{2d}_{c}(Y_{G}(K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\alpha})),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},{m}}_{L_{\mathfrak{m}}})
, we write

\omega^{\varepsilon}(f)^{\mathrm{alg}}=\frac{\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}i_{p}(\omega_{J}(W^{*}_{\mathfrak{c}p^{\alpha}}f^{-\iota}))}{i_{p}(\Omega^{\varepsilon}(f))}.

We call
\Omega^{\varepsilon}(f)\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
the period for
f
corresponding to
\varepsilon
. Similar definition can be made for Hilbert modular forms over
F
. Under the hypothesis on
\varepsilon
, this is non-zero by the discussion in
[
25
, §6]
.

Let
\mathbf{f}
be a Hida family for
F
corresponding to an irreducible component of
\mathcal{H}^{H/Z_{H}}_{11}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}L_{\mathfrak{m}}
, with idempotent
\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{f}}
. Denote by
\mathbf{f}_{E}
the base-change Hida family obtained by composing
\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{f}}
with
\mathrm{BC}
, which we assume to be made of primitive forms. Denote
\mathbf{f}_{E}^{\prime}
the component of
\mathcal{H}^{G}_{11}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}L_{\mathfrak{m}}
obtained from
\mathbf{f}_{E}
. Recall that

\Lambda_{G/H}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}\llbracket T_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})/\overline{\mathcal{E}}L_{G}({\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\rrbracket,

where
L_{G}=\{{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\alpha&\\
&\tau(\alpha)\end{smallmatrix}\big)}}\,:\,\alpha\in{\rm Res}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}/{\mathbb{Z}}}\mathbf{G}_{\rm m}\}
. Then, the idempotent
\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{f}_{E}}
acts on
\varinjlim_{r}H^{2d}_{c}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},{m}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})
. Let
f_{E}
be a classical specialisation of
\mathbf{f}_{E}
and
\mathbf{f}_{E}^{\prime}
corresponding to
P:\mathcal{H}^{G/H}_{11}\to L_{\mathfrak{m}}
and
P^{\prime}:\mathcal{H}^{G}_{11}\to\mathcal{H}^{G/H}_{11}\xrightarrow{P}L_{\mathfrak{m}}
. If the
p
-part of its adjoint character has conductor
p^{\boldsymbol{r}}
, then by Lemma
6.12
and Remark
6.13
, we have an isomorphism

\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}_{E}^{\prime}}H^{2d}_{c}(Y_{G}(K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\boldsymbol{r}})),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},{m}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})\otimes_{P^{\prime}}L_{\mathfrak{m}}\to\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}_{E}}H^{2d}_{c}(Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},{m}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})\otimes_{P}L_{\mathfrak{m}},

showing that the right hand side is one dimensional over
L_{\mathfrak{m}}
. Then a generator
\omega^{\varepsilon}(f_{E})^{\mathrm{alg}}
of
\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}_{E}}H^{2d}_{c}(Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},{m}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})\otimes_{P}L_{\mathfrak{m}}
satisfies the equality

\omega^{\varepsilon}(f_{E})^{\mathrm{alg}}=\frac{\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}i_{p}(\omega_{J}(W^{*}_{\mathfrak{c}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}}f_{E}^{-\iota}))}{i_{p}(\Omega^{\varepsilon}(f_{E}))}.

If
f_{E}
is the base change of a form
f
from
F
, then the period
\Omega^{\varepsilon}(f_{E})
differs from the product
\Omega^{+}(f)\Omega^{-}(f)
by an algebraic non-zero number, where
\Omega^{+}(f)
(resp.
\Omega^{-}(f)
) corresponds to the choice character
\varepsilon
of
\left\{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\pm 1&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}\right\}^{\Sigma_{F}}
such that

\varepsilon({\big(\begin{smallmatrix}-1&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)})_{\sigma}=1

(resp.

\varepsilon({\big(\begin{smallmatrix}-1&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)})_{\sigma}=-1),

for all
\sigma\in\Sigma_{F}
. Moreover, the ratio should be a
p
-adic unit,
[
25
, Conjecture 5.1]
, see
[
41
]
for the state of the art on this conjecture.

We shall compare families arising from our Iwasawa cohomology and those defined previously in this section.

Lemma 6.14
.

Let
f
be a primitive, nearly-ordinary and base-change Hilbert modular cusp form of weight
(\hat{\underline{k}},0)
and level
V_{\boldsymbol{r}}
.
Let
{\mathfrak{m}_{f}}
be the maximal ideal of the abstract Hecke algebra corresponding to the
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-valued system of eigenvalues of
f
. Suppose the image of the residual Galois representation of
f
is not solvable.
Let
\mathbf{f}
be the unique base change primitive Hida family passing through it valued in
\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{f}}:={\rm Frac}(\mathbf{I}_{\mathbf{f}})
, with
\mathbf{I}_{\mathbf{f}}
finite flat over an irreducible component of
\Lambda_{G/H}
, and denote by
\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{f}}
its idempotent.

(1)

We have an isomorphism of
\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{f}}
-modules

\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{f}}\mathcal{H}^{G/H}_{11}\otimes_{\Lambda_{G/H}}\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{f}}\cong\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{f}}H^{2d}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})\otimes_{\Lambda_{G/H}}\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{f}}.

(2)

The isomorphism is equivariant for the action of
\left\{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\pm 1&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}\right\}^{\Sigma_{E}}
. Moreover, for every character
\epsilon
on
\left\{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\pm 1&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}\right\}^{\Sigma_{E}}
,
\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}}H^{2d}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})\otimes_{\Lambda_{G/H}}\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{f}}
is one dimensional over
\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{f}}
.

Proof.

First, note that by Control Theorem
4.34
, there is an irreducible component of
\mathcal{H}_{\rm Iw}\otimes_{\Lambda_{G/H}}\mathrm{Frac}(\Lambda_{G/H})

that shares the same Hecke eigenvalues with
\mathbf{f}
on a dense set of points. By a slight abuse of notation, we also denote by
\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{f}}
the idempotent in
\mathcal{H}_{\rm Iw}\otimes_{\Lambda_{G/H}}\mathrm{Frac}(\Lambda_{G/H})
corresponding to this irreducible component.

By
[
8
, Theorem A and Remark 1.1.1 (3)]
that is, using an argument with the Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence if
\underline{k}\neq\underline{0}
, the cohomology of the
{\mathfrak{m}_{f}}
-isotypical component is concentrated only in middle degree.
Using this fact, one can extend
[
12
, Theorem 4.4 and 6.6]
to allow
{\mathfrak{m}_{f}}
in these theorems.
We can then use
[
13
, Theorem 3.8]
(or rather its proof, see the references therein)
to get

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{G}_{11}(\hat{\underline{k}},0)_{\mathfrak{m}_{f}}\simeq\varprojlim_{r\geq 1}e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}(Y_{G}(K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}p^{r})),\mathscr{V}^{\hat{\underline{k}},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})_{\mathfrak{m}_{f}}.

This and the map of the inverse system induced by projection from level
K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}p^{{\boldsymbol{r}}})
to
V_{{{\boldsymbol{r}}}}
induce an isomorphism

\mathcal{H}^{G/H}_{11}(\hat{\underline{k}},0)_{\mathfrak{m}_{f}}\simeq\varprojlim_{r\geq 1}e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}(Y_{G}(V_{r}),\mathscr{V}^{\hat{\underline{k}},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})_{\mathfrak{m}_{f}},

which extends to a neighbourhood of
f
. We then obtain the desired isomorphism after tensoring by
\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{f}}
, completing the proof of (1).

The equivariance for the action of
\left\{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\pm 1&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}\right\}^{\Sigma_{E}}
follows from the fact that the action of the Hecke algebra at infinity commutes with the ordinary idempotent
e^{\rm n.ord}
.
Moreover, a verbatim translation of the proof of parts (b) and (c) of
[
40
, Theorem 4.7]
shows that the moment map
{\rm mom}_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\underline{k}}
gives an isomorphism (up to enlarging
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}
so that it contains the Hecke eigenvalues of
f
)

e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})_{\mathfrak{m}_{f}}\otimes_{\Lambda_{G/H,\boldsymbol{r}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}v_{\hat{\underline{k}}}^{\rm hw}\simeq e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}(Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}}),\mathscr{V}^{\hat{\underline{k}},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})_{\mathfrak{m}_{f}}.

This implies that
\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{f}}H^{2d}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})\otimes_{\Lambda_{G/H}}\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{f}}
is finite dimensional over
\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{f}}
. Furthermore, if
\varepsilon
denotes any character of
\left\{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\pm 1&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}\right\}^{\Sigma_{E}}
and if
P:\mathcal{H}_{11}^{G/H}\to L_{\mathfrak{m}}
is the point of
\mathbf{f}
corresponding to the primitive form
f
of level
V_{\boldsymbol{r}}
, we then have

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}}H^{2d}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})\otimes_{P}L_{\mathfrak{m}}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}}H^{2d}(Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}}),\mathscr{V}^{\hat{\underline{k}},{0}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})\otimes_{P}L_{\mathfrak{m}}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}}H^{2d}_{c}(Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}}),\mathscr{V}^{\hat{\underline{k}},{0}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})\otimes_{P}L_{\mathfrak{m}}.

As
f
is primitive, by Lemma
6.12
the latter is one dimensional. This proves (2).
∎

Lemma
6.14
allows us to relate the chosen generator
\omega^{\varepsilon}(f)^{\mathrm{alg}}
of
\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}}H^{2d}_{c}(Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}}),\mathscr{V}^{\hat{\underline{k}},{0}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})\otimes_{P}L_{\mathfrak{m}}
with the specialization of a generator of
\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}}H^{2d}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})\otimes_{\Lambda_{G/H}}\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{f}}
. Fix a generator
G_{\mathbf{f}}
so that when evaluating at the point
P
corresponding to
f
, it will give the following cohomology class

(28)

\displaystyle G_{\mathbf{f}}[P]=\frac{\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}\iota_{p}(\omega_{J}(W^{*}_{\mathfrak{c}p^{\alpha}}f^{-\iota}))}{\iota_{p}(\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{p}(f))}\in\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}}H^{2d}_{c}(Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}}),\mathscr{V}^{\hat{\underline{k}},{0}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}})\otimes_{P}L_{\mathfrak{m}}

A priori,
G_{\mathbf{f}}[P]
and
\omega^{\varepsilon}(f)^{\mathrm{alg}}
are not equal, but the periods
\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{p}(f)
and
\Omega^{\varepsilon}(f)
differ by a non-zero element of
L_{\mathfrak{m}}
.

7.
Adjoint
p
-adic
L
-functions

7.1.
Rankin–Selberg integrals

In this section, we introduce the auxiliary analytic calculations used to determine the Euler factor at
p
appearing in the interpolation formula of our
p
-adic
L
-function.

Let
J
be an
F
-type for
E
and define

w_{J}=(\gamma_{\sigma})_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{E}}\in G(\mathbb{R}),\text{ where }\gamma_{\sigma}=\begin{cases}{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}-1&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}&\text{ if }\sigma\in J\\
{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}&\text{ if }\sigma\not\in J.\end{cases}

We denote by
\iota_{J}:G(\mathbb{A})\to G(\mathbb{A})
the map given by right multiplication by
w_{J}
. Note that, as explained in
[
17
, Section 6.11]
,
\iota_{J}
induces the map on
G(\mathbb{R})/K_{\infty}\to G(\mathbb{R})/K_{\infty},\,(z_{\sigma})\mapsto(z_{\sigma}^{\prime})
, where

z_{\sigma}^{\prime}=\begin{cases}z_{\sigma}&\text{ if }\sigma\in J\\
\overline{z}_{\sigma}&\text{ if }\sigma\not\in J.\end{cases}

Let
\chi_{F}
be a unitary Hecke character of
{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}_{F}
. Denote
\chi:=\chi_{F}\circ{\rm N}_{E/F}
and let
\theta
be a unitary Hecke character of
\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{E}
such that its restriction to
\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{F}
is
\chi_{F}\eta_{E/F}
. Let
\kappa=(\underline{k},0)
be a weight for
F
and let
\hat{\kappa}=(\hat{\underline{k}},0)
be the corresponding weight for
E
. For each
g\in S_{\hat{\kappa}}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}),\chi)
and
s\in{\mathbb{C}}
with
{\rm Re}(s)
sufficiently large, let

I(g,s):=\int_{F^{\times}_{+}\backslash{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}_{F,+}}\int_{F\backslash{\mathbb{A}}_{F}}(g\otimes\theta^{-1})(\iota_{J}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y&x\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right))|y|^{s}_{{\mathbb{A}}_{F}}dxd^{\times}y.

The integral
I(g,s)
, which is the key analytic input of
[
17
, Theorem 10.1]
, builds a bridge between the Asai
L
-function of
g
twisted by
\theta^{-1}
and the Hirzebruch–Zagier cycles, as it calculates the former and its residue at
s=0
is related to the value of the cohomological pairing between the cycles and
\omega_{J}(g^{-\iota})
. Recall that if
\mathfrak{b}
is the conductor of
\theta
, we denote by
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{c}\mathfrak{b}^{2}
and
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{F}
its intersection with
\mathcal{O}_{F}
.

Proposition 7.1
.

If
g
is a simultaneous eigenform for all Hecke operators,

\zeta^{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{F}}_{F}(2s+2)I(g,s)=C(s)\cdot L({\rm As}(g\otimes\theta^{-1}),s+1),

where

C(s):=\frac{|D_{F}\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(D_{E/F})^{1/2}|^{s+2}|D_{F}|^{1/2}\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{F}}\Gamma(k_{\sigma}+2+s)}{(4\pi)^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{F}}(k_{\sigma}+2+s)}}.

Proof.

This is
[
17
, Lemma 10.3]
together with (
11
).
∎

We now let
B^{\prime}
be the mirabolic subgroup of
H
, i.e. the subgroup of the upper-triangular Borel of
H
given by matrices
\{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y&x\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\}
. Also
H({\mathbb{R}})^{+}
be the identity component of
H({\mathbb{R}})
and let
B^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}})^{+}
, resp.
B^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}})^{+}
, denote the intersection of
B^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}})
, resp.
B^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}})
, with
H({\mathbb{A}})^{+}=H({\mathbb{R}})^{+}H({\mathbb{A}}_{f})
. Then, as the left Haar measure on
B^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}})
corresponds to
|y|^{-1}_{{\mathbb{A}}_{F}}dxd^{\times}y
, the integral can be written as

\displaystyle I(g,s)

\displaystyle=\int_{B^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}})+\backslash B^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}})^{+}}(g\otimes\theta^{-1})(\iota_{J}(b))|{\rm det}(b)|^{s+1}_{{\mathbb{A}}_{F}}db

(29)

\displaystyle=\int_{B^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}})+\backslash B^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}})^{+}}(g^{-\iota}\otimes\theta^{-1})(\iota_{J}(b))|{\rm det}(b)|^{s+1}_{{\mathbb{A}}_{F}}db,

where in the second equality we have used that, since
(\chi\theta^{-2}){|_{{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}_{F}}}=\chi_{F}^{2}\chi_{F}^{-2}\eta_{E/F}^{-2}=1
,

(g^{-\iota}\otimes\theta^{-1})(\iota_{J}(b))=(g\otimes\theta^{-1})({\rm det}(\iota_{J}(b))^{-1}\iota_{J}(b))=\chi^{-1}\theta^{2}(({\rm det}(\iota_{J}(b)))(g\otimes\theta^{-1})(\iota_{J}(b)).

Let
\mathcal{N}:H({\mathbb{A}})\to{\mathbb{A}}_{F}^{\times}
be the character
\mathcal{N}(h)=y
if
h=bzm
with
b=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y&\star\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in B^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}})
,
z\in Z_{H}({\mathbb{A}})
, and
m\in K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}_{F}^{\prime})K^{+}_{\infty,H}
and trivial otherwise and define the (normalized) Eisenstein series

(30)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{*}(h,s):=\zeta_{F}^{\mathfrak{c}_{F}^{\prime}}(2s)\cdot\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\gamma\in\mathcal{O}_{F}^{\times}B^{\prime}({\mathbb{Q}})\backslash H({\mathbb{Q}})}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\left|\mathcal{N}(\gamma h)\right|_{{\mathbb{A}}_{F}}^{s}.

\mathcal{E}^{*}(h,s)
is absolutely convergent for
{\rm Re}(s)>1
and can be meromorphically continued to the whole complex plane with a simple pole at
s=1
(
cf
.
[
17
, §10.2]
or
[
23
, §6]
). Following
[
25
, Appendix A]
, its residue at
s=1
is explicitly

(31)

\displaystyle{\rm Res}_{s=1}\mathcal{E}^{*}(h,s)=|(\mathcal{O}_{F}/\mathfrak{c}_{F}^{\prime})^{\times}|\frac{(2\pi)^{d}R_{F}}{2^{2}D_{F}{\rm N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{c}_{F}^{\prime})^{2}}=:C_{\mathcal{E}},

where recall that
R_{F}
is the regulator of
F
. In the following Proposition, set
\mathfrak{c}
to be stable under
\tau\in{\rm Gal}(E/F)
.

Proposition 7.2
.

If
g
is a simultaneous eigenform for all Hecke operators,

\displaystyle\langle[\omega_{J}(g^{-\iota}\otimes\theta^{-1})],\mathscr{Z}^{\hat{\underline{k}},\chi^{-1}\theta^{2}}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})\rangle=

\displaystyle\frac{C(0)}{C_{\mathcal{E}}}{\rm Res}_{s=1}L({\rm As}(g\otimes\theta^{-1}),s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\zeta^{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{F}}_{F}(2)}{C_{\mathcal{E}}}{\rm Res}_{s=0}I(g,s).

In particular,

\displaystyle\langle[\omega_{J}(g^{-\iota})],\mathscr{Z}^{\hat{\underline{k}}}(\mathfrak{c})_{\theta}\rangle=

\displaystyle\frac{[K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}):\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\ast,+}K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})]G(\theta)C(0)}{C_{\mathcal{E}}}{\rm Res}_{s=1}L({\rm As}(g\otimes\theta^{-1}),s).

Proof.

This statement is contained in the proof of
[
17
, Theorem 10.1]
and we sketch it for the convenience of the reader. Recall that we have a decomposition
H({\mathbb{A}})^{+}=H({\mathbb{Q}})^{+}B^{\prime}({\mathbb{A}})^{+}K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}_{F}^{\prime})K^{+}_{\infty,H}
. Using (
29
), the invariance of
g^{-\iota}
by right-multiplication of
K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}_{F}^{\prime})
, and unfolding
\mathcal{E}^{*}(h,s)
, we have (
cf
.
[
17
, Proposition 10.4]
)

\zeta^{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{F}}_{F}(2s+2)I(g,s)=\int_{Y_{H}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}_{F}^{\prime}))}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\mathcal{N}(\iota_{J}(h_{\infty}))^{\underline{k}+\mathbf{2}}\mathcal{E}^{*}(h,s+1){\rm det}(\iota_{J}(h_{\infty}))^{-\frac{\hat{\underline{k}}}{2}-\mathbf{1}}j(\iota_{J}(h_{\infty}),\mathbf{i})^{\hat{\underline{k}}+\mathbf{2}}(g^{-\iota}\otimes\theta^{-1})(\iota_{J}(h))dh,

with
j(\iota_{J}(h_{\infty}),\mathbf{i})
denoting the automorphy factor for
E
defined as in
[
17
, p. 57]
and
dh=dh_{\infty}dh_{f}
is the Haar measure chosen so that
dh_{f}
assigns volume 1 to
K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{F})
, while invariant measure
dh_{\infty}
on
H({\mathbb{R}})
gives measure one to
K_{\infty,H}^{+}
. By
[
17
, Proposition 9.2]
and (
31
), the residue at
s=0
of the right hand side equals to

C_{\mathcal{E}}\cdot\int_{Y_{H}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}_{F}^{\prime}))}\iota^{\hat{\underline{k}},*}(\omega_{J}(g^{-\iota}\otimes\theta^{-1})).

This and Proposition
7.1
give

\int_{Y_{H}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}_{F}^{\prime}))}\iota^{\hat{\underline{k}},*}(\omega_{J}(g^{-\iota}\otimes\theta^{-1}))=C_{1}\cdot{\rm Res}_{s=1}L({\rm As}(g\otimes\theta^{-1}),s),

where

C_{1}=\frac{C(0)}{C_{\mathcal{E}}}=\frac{|D_{F}\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(D_{E/F})^{1/2}|^{2}|D_{F}|^{1/2}\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{F}}\Gamma(k_{\sigma}+2)}{C_{\mathcal{E}}(4\pi)^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{F}}(k_{\sigma}+2)}}.

The result then follows from Lemma
3.12
.
∎

Now let
f_{E}\in S_{\hat{\kappa}}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}),\chi)
be an eigenform which is the base change to
E
of a Hilbert cuspidal eigenform
f
. Recall that if
f
is nearly ordinary at
\mathfrak{p}
, then
f_{E}
is nearly ordinary at primes of
E
above
\mathfrak{p}
.

Lemma 7.3
.

For each
\mathfrak{p}\mid p
, suppose that
\pi(f)_{\mathfrak{p}}
is unramified and that
f
is nearly-ordinary at
\mathfrak{p}
, with Satake parameters
\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}},\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Let
f_{\alpha},f_{E,\alpha}
be the corresponding
p
-stabilisations.
Then

I(f_{E},s)=\left(\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\frac{1+\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-s-1}}{(1-\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p})\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-s-1})(1-\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p})\frac{\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-s-1})}\right)I(f_{E,\alpha},s).

In particular,

{\rm Res}_{s=0}I(f_{E},s)=\left(\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\frac{1+\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-1}}{(1-\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p})\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-1})(1-\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p})\frac{\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-1})}\right){\rm Res}_{s=0}I(f_{E,\alpha},s)

Proof.

By
[
17
, Lemma 10.3]
, we need to compare

\sum_{\mathfrak{n}\subset\mathcal{O}_{F}}\frac{a(\mathfrak{n}\mathcal{O}_{E},f_{E}\otimes\theta^{-1})}{\mathrm{N}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{n})^{s+2}}

and

\sum_{\mathfrak{n}\subset\mathcal{O}_{F}}\frac{a(\mathfrak{n}\mathcal{O}_{E},f_{E,\alpha}\otimes\theta^{-1})}{\mathrm{N}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{n})^{s+2}}.

By the Euler product expansion in
[
17
, (10.2.2)]
9
9

9

Note that in
[
17
, (10.2.2)]
and the line above there is a typo as the summation over
j
starts from
j=0
and not
j=1
.
of these two series, this reduces to a calculation at each prime
\mathfrak{p}
of
F
above
p
. Notice that the Euler factor at
\mathfrak{p}
of the latter series is

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}a(\mathfrak{p}^{j}\mathcal{O}_{E},f_{E,\alpha}\otimes\theta^{-1})\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-js-2j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2j}\theta^{-j}(\mathfrak{p})\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-js-j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2j}(\chi_{F}\eta_{E/F})^{-j}(\mathfrak{p})\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-js-j}

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2}(\chi_{F}\eta_{E/F})^{-1}(\mathfrak{p})\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-s-1})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(1-\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p})\tfrac{\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-s-1})^{-1},

where we have used that
a(\mathfrak{p}^{j}\mathcal{O}_{E},f_{E,\alpha})=\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}\mathcal{O}_{E}}(f_{E})^{j}\mathrm{N}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{j/2}=\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}^{2j}\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{j}
and that
\chi_{F}(\mathfrak{p})=\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}
. On the other hand, the Euler factor at
\mathfrak{p}
of the former power series, which is

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}a(\mathfrak{p}^{j}\mathcal{O}_{E},f_{E}\otimes\theta^{-1})\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-js-2j},

equals to (
[
17
, (10.2.5)]
)

\frac{L_{\mathfrak{p}}(\mathrm{As}(f_{E}\otimes\theta^{-1}),s+1)}{\zeta_{F,\mathfrak{p}}(2s+2)}.

Thanks to Proposition
3.7
, this further reads as

\frac{L_{\mathfrak{p}}(\mathrm{Ad}(f)\otimes\eta_{E/F},s+1)\zeta_{F,\mathfrak{p}}(s+1)}{\zeta_{F,\mathfrak{p}}(2s+2)},

which is explicitly

\frac{1+\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-s-1}}{(1-\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p})\frac{\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-s-1})(1-\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p})\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-s-1})(1-\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p})\frac{\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-s-1})}.

We thus conclude by comparing the two factors at
\mathfrak{p}
. The formula on the residues follows from this and from the fact that

(32)

\displaystyle\frac{1+\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-s-1}}{(1-\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p})\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-s-1})(1-\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p})\frac{\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-s-1})}

has a pole at
s=0
if and only if

\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}=\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p})\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p}).

As
\mathfrak{p}
is spherical unramified for
\pi(f)
,
\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}
are Weil numbers of the same weight (
cf
.
[
5
, Theorem 1]
), thus taking any complex absolute value we get a contradiction, implying that (
32
) has no pole at
s=0
.
∎

For any
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}
and
r\geq 0
, let
\gamma_{\mathfrak{p},r}\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(E_{\mathfrak{p}})
be the matrix given by

\gamma_{\mathfrak{p},r}:=\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-r}u_{\mathfrak{p}}\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{r}=\begin{cases}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-r}\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)&\text{ if }\mathfrak{p}\text{ is inert in }E,\\
\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-r}\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)&\text{ if }\mathfrak{p}\text{ is split in }E.\end{cases}

Given
\boldsymbol{r}=(r_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{p}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{|\Upsilon_{F,p}|}
, we also let
\gamma_{\boldsymbol{r}}
be the matrix with
\mathfrak{p}
-component
\gamma_{\mathfrak{p},r_{\mathfrak{p}}}
and
1
elsewhere.

Let
f_{E,\alpha}
be a nearly ordinary base change cusp form of weight
\hat{\kappa}
, level
V_{\boldsymbol{r}}
, and
U_{\mathfrak{P}}
-eigenvalue
\alpha_{\mathfrak{P}}{\rm N}_{E/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{P})^{1/2}
. This implies that the conductor of the
\mathfrak{P}
-part of the adjoint character
\chi^{\prime}
is at most
\mathfrak{P}^{r_{\mathfrak{p}}}
for
\mathfrak{P}
lying above
\mathfrak{p}
. In the following we can and do choose
\boldsymbol{r}
minimal so that
\chi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}
is of conductor exactly
\mathfrak{P}^{r_{\mathfrak{p}}}
. This means that
f_{E,\alpha}\in S_{\hat{\kappa}}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}),\chi,\chi^{\prime})
, with
\mathfrak{c}
coprime with
p\mathcal{O}_{E}
.

Lemma 7.4
.

For any
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}
, suppose that the
\mathfrak{p}
-component of
\chi_{F}
is spherical and let
\mathfrak{P}
be a prime of
E
above
\mathfrak{p}
.
Suppose that
f_{E,\alpha}
has level
V_{\boldsymbol{r}}
, with minimal
\boldsymbol{r}
.

(1)

If
\pi(f_{E,\alpha})_{\mathfrak{P}}
is either an unramified principal series or Steinberg, then

I(\gamma_{\mathfrak{p},r_{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot f_{E,\alpha},s)=I(f_{E,\alpha},s).

(2)

If
\pi(f_{E,\alpha})_{\mathfrak{P}}
is a ramified principal series, then

I(\gamma_{\mathfrak{p},r_{\mathfrak{p}}}\cdot f_{E,\alpha},s)=G(\chi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime})I(f_{E,\alpha},s),

where
G(\chi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime})
denotes the Gauss sum of the restriction to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}
of
\chi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}
.

Remark 7.5
.

Note that if both
\mathfrak{P}
and
\mathfrak{Q}
are primes of
E
which lie above
\mathfrak{p}
, then
\pi(f_{E,\alpha})_{\mathfrak{P}}
and
\pi(f_{E,\alpha})_{\mathfrak{Q}}
are of the same type (and indeed isomorphic).

Proof of Lemma
7.4
.

As in Lemma
7.3
, we need to compare the Euler factors at
\mathfrak{p}
of the two series given by
[
17
, Lemma 10.3]
. We prove the Lemma in the case when
\mathfrak{p}
is inert in
E
, with the split case following similarly. By abusing notation, we also call
\mathfrak{p}
the prime for
E
above it. We distinguish two cases.

Suppose that
\pi(f_{E,\alpha})_{\mathfrak{p}}
is either an unramified principal series or Steinberg (which correspond to the cases (UnrPS) and (St) of §
6.2
). Then
r:=r_{\mathfrak{p}}=0
and we notice that the Fourier coefficients of
\gamma_{\mathfrak{p},0}\cdot f_{E,\alpha}=f_{E,\alpha}(\,\cdot\gamma_{\mathfrak{p},0})
are the same as those of
f_{E,\alpha}
, hence
I(\gamma_{\mathfrak{p},0}\cdot f_{E,\alpha},s)=I(f_{E,\alpha},s)
. As
\mathfrak{p}
is inert in
E
, then, for
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y&x\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(F_{\mathfrak{p}})
, we have that

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y&x\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\gamma_{\mathfrak{p},0}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y&x+y\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right),

and
x+y\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}
is always integral when
x,y
are, proving that the Fourier expansion (
5
) of
\gamma_{\mathfrak{p},0}\cdot f_{E,\alpha}
equals the one of
f_{E,\alpha}
, hence that the two Euler factors at
\mathfrak{p}
agree.

Suppose
\pi(f_{E,\alpha})_{\mathfrak{p}}
is a ramified principal series (which corresponds to the case (RamPS) of §
6.2
) with the induction of the characters
({\chi^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}},{\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}\chi^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{-1})
, and note that by the definition of
V_{\boldsymbol{r}}
the restriction to
\mathcal{O}_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{\times}
of the character
\chi^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}
has conductor (at most)
\mathfrak{p}^{r}
, and by hypothesis we suppose that its conductor is exactly
\mathfrak{p}^{r}
(otherwise we choose a smaller
r
at the beginning).

We calculate the local integral by making use of the explicit formulae for the Whittaker model of
\pi(f_{E,\alpha})_{\mathfrak{p}}
. Indeed, by
[
17
, Eq. (D.5.9)]
the Fourier coefficients are calculated via the Whittaker functions is a tensor product of local Whittaker functions (see Theorem D.1 of
loc. cit.
).
Subsequently, we perform calculations inspired by
[
28
, §5.3]
.
Let
W^{\mathrm{ord}}_{f_{E,\alpha},\mathfrak{p}}
be the unique (up to scalar) ordinary vector in the local Whittaker model at
\mathfrak{p}
of
\pi(f_{E,\alpha})_{\mathfrak{p}}
, normalised as in
[
28
, Corollary 2.3]
. In particular, by
loc. cit.
, if the local representation is a principal series associated with the induction of the characters
({\chi^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}},{\chi^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{-1}\chi_{\mathfrak{p}})
, the local Whittaker model satisfies, for
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y&x\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(E_{\mathfrak{p}}),

W^{\mathrm{ord}}_{f_{E,\alpha},\mathfrak{p}}\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y&x\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)=e_{\mathfrak{p}}(x)W^{\mathrm{ord}}_{f_{E,\alpha},\mathfrak{p}}\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)=\begin{cases}e_{\mathfrak{p}}(x)\chi^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}(y)|y|_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{1/2}&\text{if }y\in E_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}\cap\mathcal{O}_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}},\\
0&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

where
e_{\mathfrak{p}}(-)
is the local additive character defined in Section
3.1.2
.
Recall that with the normalisation in Section
3.2.1
above, in this case with
f_{E,\alpha}
and
y=\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}
, the value
\chi^{\prime}(\pi_{\mathfrak{p}})
is
\alpha(f_{E,\alpha})_{1,\mathfrak{p}}
and
\alpha(f_{E,\alpha})_{1,\mathfrak{p}}|\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}|_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{1/2}
is exactly equal to
a(\mathfrak{p}\mathcal{O}_{E},f_{E,\alpha})
. Also, notice that when we twist the form
f_{E,\alpha}
by
\theta^{-1}
, the
\mathfrak{p}
-component of the associated automorphic representation is
\pi(f_{E,\alpha})_{\mathfrak{p}}\otimes\theta^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}
, which has Satake parameters
\theta^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\pi_{\mathfrak{p}})\alpha(f_{E,\alpha})_{1,\mathfrak{p}}
,
\theta^{-1}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\pi_{\mathfrak{p}})\alpha(f_{E,\alpha})_{2,\mathfrak{p}}
. Recall that, by hypothesis, the central character
\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}
of
\pi(f_{E,\alpha})_{\mathfrak{p}}
is unramified and the restriction to
F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}
of
\theta_{\mathfrak{p}}
agrees with
\chi_{F,\mathfrak{p}}\eta_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}/F_{\mathfrak{p}}}
, hence it is unramified as well.

We now evaluate the local integral at
\mathfrak{p}
given in the proof of
[
17
, Lemma 10.3]
of the Rankin–Selberg integral
I(\gamma_{\mathfrak{p},r}\cdot f_{E,\alpha},s)
. Note that the integral is over
F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}
but we do use the Whittaker model at
\mathfrak{p}
of the base change
f_{E,\alpha}\otimes\theta^{-1}
.
All steps are the same as in the proof of
[
17
, Lemma 10.3]
till the top of page 162. Consider an element
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y&x\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(F_{\mathfrak{p}})
. We shall use the following equality:
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y&x\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\gamma_{\mathfrak{p},r}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y&y\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-r}\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}+x\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right).

To lighten notation, we let
g=f_{E,\alpha}\otimes\theta^{-1}
in the following calculation:

\displaystyle\int_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}}a(y,\gamma_{\mathfrak{p},r}\cdot g)|y|_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{s+2}{\textup{d}^{\times}y}=

\displaystyle\int_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}}W_{g,\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{ord}}(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\gamma_{\mathfrak{p},r})|y|_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{s+2}{\textup{d}^{\times}y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}}W_{g,\mathfrak{p}}^{\mathrm{ord}}(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}y&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right))e_{\mathfrak{p}}(y\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-r}\delta_{\mathfrak{p}})|y|_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{s+2}{\textup{d}^{\times}y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}\cap\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(\theta_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}\chi^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}})(y)|y|^{1/2}_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}}e_{\mathfrak{p}}(y\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-r}\delta_{\mathfrak{p}})|y|_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{s+2}{\textup{d}^{\times}y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n\geq 0}\eta_{E/F}^{n}(\mathfrak{p})(\chi_{F,\mathfrak{p}}^{-1}{\chi^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}})^{n}(\pi_{\mathfrak{p}})|\pi^{n}_{\mathfrak{p}}|_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{s+3}\int_{\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\chi^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}}(y)e_{\mathfrak{p}}(y\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{n-r}\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}){\textup{d}^{\times}y},

where we have used that the restriction to
F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}
of
\theta_{\mathfrak{p}}
agrees with
\chi_{F,\mathfrak{p}}\eta_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}/F_{\mathfrak{p}}}
.
Recall that
\pi(f_{E,\alpha})_{\mathfrak{p}}
is a principal series associated to the characters
(\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime},{\chi^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{-1}\chi_{\mathfrak{p}})
, with
\chi^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{p}}
a character on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}
with exponent of the conductor equal to
r
. Recall also that, as
f_{E,\alpha}
