is a base change form,
\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}=\chi_{F,\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}\circ{\rm N}_{E_{\mathfrak{p}}/F_{\mathfrak{p}}}
and the exponent of the conductor of
\chi_{F,\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
equals
r
by
[
17
, Proposition E.9]
. Then the restriction to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}
of
\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
has conductor
\mathfrak{p}^{r}
and the inner integral is

\int_{\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{\mathfrak{p}}}\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}(y)e_{\mathfrak{p}}(y\pi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{n-r}\delta_{\mathfrak{p}}){\textup{d}^{\times}y}=\begin{cases}G(\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime})&\text{if }n=0\\
0&\text{ if }n>0,\end{cases}

by for instance
[
35
, Lemma 7-4]
. Plugging this in, we have that

\displaystyle\int_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}}a(y,\gamma_{\mathfrak{p},r}\cdot g)|y|_{F_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{s+2}{\textup{d}^{\times}y}=

\displaystyle G(\chi_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}).

Note that in this case the local
L
-factor at
\mathfrak{p}
is
1
for

I(f_{E,\alpha},s)
.

∎

7.2.
A Hecke-equivariant pairing

We keep the notation used in §
7.1
. In particular, we let
\chi_{F}
be a unitary Hecke character of
{\mathbb{A}}_{F}^{\times}
,
\chi:=\chi_{F}\circ{\rm N}_{E/F}
and let
\theta
be a unitary Hecke character of
{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}
such that its restriction to
{\mathbb{A}}_{F}^{\times}
is
\chi_{F}\eta_{E/F}
. Set also
\mathfrak{c}
to be coprime to
p\mathcal{O}_{E}
and stable under
\tau\in{\rm Gal}(E/F)
.

Note that the pairing in intersection cohomology
\langle\,,\,\rangle_{IH}
and the Poincaré pairing
\langle\,,\,\rangle
introduced in §
3.3
for the variety
Y_{G}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}))
are not Hecke-equivariant for
T_{\eta}
. Define

(33)

\displaystyle[-,-]=\langle-,W^{*}_{\mathfrak{c}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}}-\rangle.

To ease the notation, let
\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}
denote the big cycle
\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}^{\chi^{-1}\theta^{2}}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})\in e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi^{-1}\theta^{2}))
, with
\chi^{-1}\theta^{2}
supposed to be unramified at
p
. Similarly, the class at finite level
V_{{\boldsymbol{r}}}
will be denoted by
\mathscr{Z}_{\boldsymbol{r}}
. In what follows, to highlight its level, we also denote by
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}}^{*}(h,s)
the normalized Eisenstein series of level
K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}_{F}^{\prime}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}})
introduced in (
30
).

Lemma 7.6
.

For
\boldsymbol{r}=(r_{\mathfrak{p}})_{\mathfrak{p}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}^{|\Upsilon_{F,p}|}
, we let
f_{E,\alpha}\in S_{(\underline{k},0)}(K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}),\chi,\chi^{\prime})
be a nearly ordinary form such that it has level
V_{\boldsymbol{r}}
and it is the base change of
f_{\alpha}
. We suppose that the Nebentypus
\chi_{F}
of
f_{\alpha}
has trivial component at
\mathfrak{p}
for any
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}
and that
\chi^{-1}\theta^{2}
is unramified at
p
. Set
f=f_{E,\alpha}\otimes\theta^{-1}
and let
\gamma_{\boldsymbol{r}}
be the matrix with
\mathfrak{p}
-component
\gamma_{\mathfrak{p},r_{\mathfrak{p}}}
and
1
elsewhere.

(1)

If
r_{\mathfrak{p}}\geq 1
for all
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}
, we have

[\omega_{J}(W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}}^{*}f^{-\iota}),\mathrm{mom}^{\underline{k}}_{\boldsymbol{r}}\,\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}]=\frac{1}{{\alpha^{\circ}_{p}}^{2\boldsymbol{r}}}\frac{\zeta^{p\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{F}}_{F}(2)}{a_{\underline{k}}C_{\mathcal{E},\boldsymbol{r}}}{\rm Res}_{s=0}I(\gamma_{\boldsymbol{r}}\cdot f_{E,\alpha},s),

where
a_{\underline{k}}
is the constant of Lemma
4.6
,
C_{\mathcal{E},\boldsymbol{r}}=\mathrm{Res}_{s=1}\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}}^{*}(h,s)
, and
{\alpha^{\circ}_{p}}^{2\boldsymbol{r}}=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}}{\alpha^{\circ}_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{2r_{\mathfrak{p}}}
, with
\alpha^{\circ}_{\mathfrak{p}}
the
U_{\mathfrak{p}}^{{}^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}}
-eigenvalue of
f_{\alpha}
.

(2)

If
\boldsymbol{r}=\boldsymbol{0}
, then we have

[\omega_{J}(W_{\mathfrak{c}p}^{*}\,f^{-\iota}),\mathrm{mom}^{\underline{k}}_{0}\,\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}]=\mathcal{E}^{\boldsymbol{0}}_{p}(f_{\alpha})\frac{\zeta^{p\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{F}}_{F}(2)}{a_{\underline{k}}C_{\mathcal{E},\boldsymbol{0}}}{\rm Res}_{s=0}I(\gamma_{\boldsymbol{0}}\cdot f_{E,\alpha},s),

with
\mathcal{E}^{\boldsymbol{0}}_{p}(f_{\alpha}):=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}\left(1-p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}(1+k_{\sigma})}\,\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p}){\alpha^{\circ}_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{-2}\right)
.

Proof.

We suppose for simplicity that
\boldsymbol{r}=(r)_{\mathfrak{p}}
and that
\chi^{-1}\theta^{2}
is trivial, with the general case having identical proof but considerably heavier notation.

Recall that, for
r\geq 1
,

\mathrm{mom}^{\underline{k}}_{r}\,\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}=T_{\eta}^{-r}e^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}\,\mathscr{Z}_{r}^{\underline{k}}=T_{\eta}^{-r}e^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}((\eta^{r})_{\ast}z_{r}^{\underline{k}}),

with

z_{r}^{\underline{k}}=u_{\star}\circ{\iota_{r}}_{\star}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{Y_{H}(uK_{r}u^{-1}\cap H)})\in H^{2d}(Y_{G}(K_{r}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},0}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}).

As
T_{\eta}
is self-adjoint for the pairing
[\,,\,]
, we have

\displaystyle[\omega_{J}(W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}}^{*}f^{-\iota}),\mathrm{mom}^{\underline{k}}_{r}\,\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}]

\displaystyle=\langle\omega_{J}(W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}}^{*}f^{-\iota}),W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}}^{*}T_{\eta}^{-r}e^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}(\eta^{r})_{\ast}u_{\star}\circ{\iota_{r}}_{\star}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{Y_{H}(uK_{r}u^{-1}\cap H)})\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle T_{\eta}^{-r}e^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}\omega_{J}(W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}}^{*}f^{-\iota}),W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}}^{*}(\eta^{r})_{\ast}u_{\star}\circ{\iota_{r}}_{\star}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{Y_{H}(uK_{r}u^{-1}\cap H)})\rangle

(Proposition
6.2
)

\displaystyle=\langle\omega_{J}(W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}}^{*}(e^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}{U_{p}^{{}^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}}}^{-r}f)^{-\iota}),W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}}^{*}(\eta^{r})_{\ast}u_{\star}\circ{\iota_{r}}_{\star}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{Y_{H}(uK_{r}u^{-1}\cap H)})\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\alpha_{p}^{\circ}}^{2r}}\langle\omega_{J}(W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}}^{*}f^{-\iota}),W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}}^{*}(\eta^{r})_{\ast}u_{\star}\circ{\iota_{r}}_{\star}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{Y_{H}(uK_{r}u^{-1}\cap H)})\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\alpha_{p}^{\circ}}^{2r}}\langle\omega_{J}(W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}}^{*}f^{-\iota}),W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}}^{*}\gamma_{r,\ast}(\eta^{r})_{\ast}\circ{\iota_{r}}_{\star}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{Y_{H}(uK_{r}u^{-1}\cap H)})\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\alpha_{p}^{\circ}}^{2r}}\langle\omega_{J}(W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}}^{*}f^{-\iota}),({}^{t}u^{-1})_{\ast}W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}}^{*}(\eta^{r})_{\ast}\circ{\iota_{r}}_{\star}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{Y_{H}(uK_{r}u^{-1}\cap H)})\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\alpha_{p}^{\circ}}^{2r}}\langle({}^{t}u^{-1})^{\ast}\omega_{J}(W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}}^{*}f^{-\iota}),W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}}^{*}(\eta^{r})_{\ast}\circ{\iota_{r}}_{\star}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{Y_{H}(uK_{r}u^{-1}\cap H)})\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\alpha_{p}^{\circ}}^{2r}}\langle\omega_{J}(W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}}^{*}(\gamma_{r}\cdot f)^{-\iota}),W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}}^{*}(\eta^{r})_{\ast}\circ{\iota_{r}}_{\star}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{Y_{H}(uK_{r}u^{-1}\cap H)})\rangle

where we have used that the
U_{\mathfrak{p}}^{{}^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}}
-eigenvalue of
f_{E,\alpha}
is the square of the
U_{\mathfrak{p}}^{{}^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}}
-eigenvalue of
f_{\alpha}
, and where, in the sixth equality, we have used the adjointness of pushforward and pullback for Poincaré duality.
By the proof of Lemma 7.8 in
[
17
]
10
10

10

We observe that the power
[k+2m]/2
in
loc. cit.
corresponds to our
m
.
and the compatibility between Petersson product and Poincaré pairing, we have

\langle W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}}^{*}\clubsuit,W_{\mathfrak{c}p^{r+1}}^{*}\diamondsuit\rangle=\mathrm{N}_{E/\mathbb{Q}}(\mathfrak{c}p^{r})^{m}\langle\clubsuit,\diamondsuit\rangle=\langle\clubsuit,\diamondsuit\rangle,

as we have supposed
m=0
. Combining this with the commutative diagram (
3.1.4
) we are left to evaluate

(34)

\displaystyle\langle\omega_{J}((\gamma_{r}\cdot f)^{-\iota}),(\eta^{r})_{\ast}\circ{\iota_{r}}_{\star}\circ\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\mathbf{1}_{Y_{H}(uK_{r}u^{-1}\cap H)})\rangle

Now, recall that as
\eta\in H({\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, the maps
(\eta^{r})_{\ast}
and
{\iota_{r}}_{\star}
commute. Then
(\eta^{r})_{\ast}
defines an isomorphism between
\mathbf{1}_{Y_{H}(uK_{r}u^{-1}\cap H)}
and
\mathbf{1}_{Y_{H}(\gamma_{r}V_{r}\gamma_{r}^{-1}\cap H)}
and

(
34
)

\displaystyle=\int_{Y_{H}(\gamma_{r}V_{r}\gamma_{r}^{-1}\cap H)}\iota^{\dagger,\underline{k},\star}\left(\omega_{J}(\gamma_{r}\cdot f^{-\iota})\right)

(Proposition
7.2
)

\displaystyle=\frac{\zeta^{p\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{F}}_{F}(2)}{a_{\underline{k}}C_{\mathcal{E},\boldsymbol{r}}}{\rm Res}_{s=0}I(\gamma_{r}\cdot f,s),

where
a_{\underline{k}}
is the constant of Lemma
4.6
used to re-normalize the branching map. This completes the proof of the case when
r\geq 1
.

If
r=0
, by Corollary
5.7
, we have

\mathrm{mom}^{\underline{k}}_{0}\,\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}\left(1-q_{\mathfrak{p}}\cdot p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}k_{\sigma}}\,T_{\eta_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{-1}\right)\cdot e^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}\mathscr{Z}_{0}^{\underline{k}}.

Using that the pairing is Hecke equivariant and Proposition
6.2
, which says that
T_{\eta}
corresponds to
U_{p}^{{}^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}}
, we need to calculate

\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}\left(1-q_{\mathfrak{p}}\cdot p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}k_{\sigma}}\,{U_{\mathfrak{p}}^{{}^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}}}^{-1}\right)f_{E,\alpha}\otimes\theta^{-1}

which is

\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}\left(1-p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}(1+k_{\sigma})}\,\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p}){\alpha^{\circ}_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{-2}\right)f_{E,\alpha}\otimes\theta^{-1},

where we used

, that the
U_{\mathfrak{p}}^{{}^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}}
-eigenvalue of
f_{E,\alpha}
is the square of the
U_{\mathfrak{p}}^{{}^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}}
-eigenvalue of
f_{\alpha}
, and that the restriction of

to
{\mathbb{A}}_{F}^{\times}
is
\eta_{E/F}\chi_{F}
, with
\chi_{F}(\mathfrak{p})=1
.
∎

For simplicity, here we did not consider the intermediate cases where
\boldsymbol{r}
is zero on any possible proper subset of
\Upsilon_{F,p}
, however it can be done by adding the appropriate Euler factor which appears in Remark
5.8
.

7.3.
The adjoint
p
-adic
L
-function

We let
\chi_{F}
be a unitary Hecke character of
{\mathbb{A}}_{F}^{\times}
which has trivial component at every place of
F
above
p
. We let
\chi:=\chi_{F}\circ{\rm N}_{E/F}
and let
\theta
be a unitary Hecke character of
{\mathbb{A}}_{E}^{\times}
with conductor
\mathfrak{b}
coprime with
p
and such that its restriction to
{\mathbb{A}}_{F}^{\times}
is
\chi_{F}\eta_{E/F}
. We also let
\mathfrak{b}_{F}
denote
\mathfrak{b}\cap\mathcal{O}_{F}
. If
C_{\mathcal{E}}
denotes the number defined in (
31
) for
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{c}\mathfrak{b}^{2}
and
a_{\underline{k}}
is the constant of Lemma
4.6
, we define

\displaystyle C_{\underline{k},\theta}^{E/F}:=\frac{|D_{F}\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(D_{E/F})^{1/2}|^{2}|D_{F}|^{1/2}[K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}):\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\ast,+}K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})]G(\theta)}{a_{\underline{k}}\pi^{d}2^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{F}}(k_{\sigma}+2)}C_{\mathcal{E}}}.

In the following, suppose that
L_{\mathfrak{m}}
contains the values of
\theta
. Moreover, if
V_{\boldsymbol{r}}=K_{11}(\mathfrak{c})\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}V_{\mathfrak{p},r_{\mathfrak{p}}}
,
V_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\prime}=K_{0}(\mathfrak{c})\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}V_{\mathfrak{p},r_{\mathfrak{p}}}
, and
\tilde{{\rm pr}}:Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}})\to Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}}^{\prime})
denotes the natural degeneracy map, we consider the pullback
\mathscr{Z}^{\flat}_{\infty,\theta}:=\tilde{{\rm pr}}^{*}\mathscr{Z}_{\infty,\theta}\in e^{\rm n.ord}H^{2d}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})
of the big cycle
\mathscr{Z}_{\infty,\theta}
(twisted by
\theta
as in Definition
3.11
).

Let
\mathbf{f}
be a primitive Hida family with tame character
\chi_{F}
, which we recall being an irreducible component of
\mathcal{H}^{H/Z_{H}}_{11}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}}}L_{\mathfrak{m}},
and let
\mathbf{f}_{E}
be its base change to
E
as given in Definition
6.9
. We denote by
\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{f}}
their field of coefficients. Note that
\mathbf{f}_{E}
has tame character
\chi
. Fix a type
J
for
E/F
, let
\varepsilon
be the corresponding character on
\left\{{\big(\begin{smallmatrix}\pm 1&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\big)}\right\}^{\Sigma_{E}}
, and denote by
\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}_{E}}
the idempotent corresponding to
\mathbf{f}_{E}
and
\varepsilon
. For a primitive Hida family
\mathbf{f}
with image of the residual Galois representation not solvable, we can give the following :

Definition 7.7
.

Let
G_{\mathbf{f}_{E}}
be the generator of
\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}_{E}}H^{2d}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})\otimes_{\Lambda_{G/H}}\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{f}}
chosen after Lemma
6.14
, so that when evaluating at a point, it satisfies (
28
).
Then there exists a unique element of
\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{f}}
, that we denote by
L_{p}(\mathrm{Ad}(\mathbf{f})\otimes\eta_{E/F})
, so that we can write

\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}_{E}}\mathscr{Z}^{\flat}_{\infty,\theta}=L_{p}(\mathrm{Ad}(\mathbf{f})\otimes\eta_{E/F})G_{\mathbf{f}_{E}}.

We call
L_{p}(\mathrm{Ad}(\mathbf{f})\otimes\eta_{E/F})
the adjoint
p
-adic
L
-function of
\mathbf{f}
at
s=1
because of the following interpolation property.

Theorem 7.8
.

Let
\mathbf{f}
be a primitive Hida family with tame character
\chi_{F}
with image of the residual Galois representation not solvable.
For a point
P
of
\mathbf{f}_{E}
corresponding to a cusp form
f_{E,\alpha}
of weight
(\underline{k},0)
, minimal level
V_{\boldsymbol{r}}
, and base change of
f_{\alpha}
, we let
g=f_{E,\alpha}\otimes\theta^{-1}
and distinguish the following cases.

•

If
\pi(f_{E,\alpha})_{\mathfrak{P}}
is a ramified principal series with adjoint character
\chi^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{P}}
of conductor
\mathfrak{P}^{r_{\mathfrak{p}}}
for
\mathfrak{P}|\mathfrak{p}
, with
r_{\mathfrak{p}}\geq 1
, at each
\mathfrak{P}
, then
L_{p}(\mathrm{Ad}(\mathbf{f})\otimes\eta_{E/F})[P]
equals to

\frac{G(\chi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime})\prod_{\mathfrak{p}}{\rm N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{2r_{\mathfrak{p}}+2}{\rm Res}_{s=1}\zeta_{F}^{\mathfrak{d}_{E/F}p\mathfrak{c}_{F}\mathfrak{b}_{F}}(s)\prod_{\mathfrak{q}|\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}}L_{\mathfrak{q}}({\rm As}(g),1)C_{\underline{k},\theta}^{E/F}}{{\alpha^{\circ}_{p}}^{2\boldsymbol{r}}|(\mathcal{O}_{F}/p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}})^{\times}|}\cdot\frac{L^{\ast,\mathfrak{d}_{E/F}\mathfrak{b}_{F}}(\mathrm{Ad}(f_{\alpha})\otimes\eta_{E/F},1)}{\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{p}(f_{E,\alpha})},

with
\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}:=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{q}|\mathfrak{d}_{E/F}\\
\mathfrak{q}\nmid\mathfrak{b}_{F}\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{q}
.

•

If
\pi(f_{E,\alpha})_{\mathfrak{P}}
is Steinberg at each
\mathfrak{P}
and
f_{\alpha}
has
U_{\mathfrak{p}}
-eigenvalue
\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}{\rm N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{1/2}
for each
\mathfrak{p}
, then
L_{p}(\mathrm{Ad}(\mathbf{f})\otimes\eta_{E/F})[P]
equals to

\frac{\prod_{\mathfrak{p}|p}\left(1-\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p})\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-2}\right)p^{2d}{\rm Res}_{s=1}\zeta_{F}^{\mathfrak{d}_{E/F}p\mathfrak{c}_{F}\mathfrak{b}_{F}}(s)\prod_{\mathfrak{q}|\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}}L_{\mathfrak{q}}({\rm As}(g),1)C_{\underline{k},\theta}^{E/F}}{|(\mathcal{O}_{F}/p)^{\times}|}\cdot\frac{L^{\ast,\mathfrak{d}_{E/F}\mathfrak{b}_{F}}(\mathrm{Ad}(f_{\alpha})\otimes\eta_{E/F},1)}{\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{p}(f_{E,\alpha})}.

•

If
\pi(f_{E,\alpha})_{\mathfrak{P}}
is an unramified principal series at each
\mathfrak{P}
, then
f_{\alpha}
is the
p
-stabilisation of a nearly-ordinary form
f
at each
\mathfrak{p}
with Satake parameters
\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}},\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
L_{p}(\mathrm{Ad}(\mathbf{f})\otimes\eta_{E/F})[P]
is

\displaystyle\frac{\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}(1-\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p})\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-2})(1-\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p})\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-1})(1-\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p})\frac{\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-1})}{|(\mathcal{O}_{F}/p)^{\times}|}

\displaystyle p^{2d}\cdot

\displaystyle{\rm Res}_{s=1}\zeta_{F}^{\mathfrak{d}_{E/F}p\mathfrak{c}_{F}\mathfrak{b}_{F}}(s)\prod_{\mathfrak{q}|\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}}L_{\mathfrak{q}}({\rm As}(f_{E}),1)C_{\underline{k},\theta}^{E/F}

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{L^{\ast,\mathfrak{d}_{E/F}\mathfrak{b}_{F}}(\mathrm{Ad}(f)\otimes\eta_{E/F},1)}{\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{p}(f_{E,\alpha})}

Proof.

The proof revolves around calculating explicitly
\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}_{E}}\mathscr{Z}_{\infty,\theta}^{\flat}
in terms of the fixed basis
G_{\mathbf{f}_{E}}
. To calculate the coefficient, we use the Hecke-equivariant Poincaré pairing
[\phantom{e},\phantom{e}]
defined in Section
7.2
, then use the integral presentation of the adjoint
L
-value (see Proposition
7.2
) as well as the zeta integral computations in §
7.1
. Recall that both
\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}_{E}}
and
[\phantom{e},\phantom{e}]
are
U_{p}
-equivariant.

Using that the evaluation of
G_{\mathbf{f}_{E}}
at a point
P
, corresponding to a form
f_{E,\alpha}
of weight
(\underline{k},0)
and level
V_{\boldsymbol{r}}
, satisfies (
28
), the
p
-adic
L
-function at
P
is explicitly

L_{p}(\mathrm{Ad}(\mathbf{f})\otimes\eta_{E/F})[P]=\frac{[\omega_{J}(W^{*}_{\mathfrak{c}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}}f_{E,\alpha}^{-\iota}),{\rm mom}^{\underline{k}}_{\boldsymbol{r}}\,\mathscr{Z}_{\infty,\theta}^{\flat}]}{\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{p}({f}_{E,\alpha})}.

Indeed, the evaluation at
P
factors through the moment map of Definition
4.29
as the specialisation map to weight
(\underline{k},0)
is given by taking the cup product with the highest weight vector: by
[
21
, Corollary 3.4 and Theorem 3.3]
the map
j_{\ast}
of
[
20
, (8.2b)]
, which is the cup product with the highest weight vector, is the inverse (on the ordinary part) of the projection to the highest weight vector.
As
\tilde{{\rm pr}}^{*}
and the correspondence
\mathcal{P}_{\mathfrak{b}}
(introduced before Definition
3.11
) commute with
{\rm mom}^{\underline{k}}_{\boldsymbol{r}}
, we have (by
[
17
, Proposition 9.5]
- see also Proposition
7.2
)

(35)

\displaystyle L_{p}(\mathrm{Ad}(\mathbf{f})\otimes\eta_{E/F})[P]=[K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}):\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\ast,+}K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})]G(\theta)\frac{[\omega_{J}(W^{*}_{\mathfrak{c}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}}g^{-\iota}),{\rm mom}^{\underline{k}}_{\boldsymbol{r}}\,\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}^{\chi^{-1}\theta^{2}}]}{\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{p}({f}_{E,\alpha})},

where, up to possibly enlarge
L
so that
L_{\mathfrak{m}}
contains the values of
\theta
, we have

\frac{\omega_{J}(W^{*}_{\mathfrak{c}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}}g^{-\iota})}{\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{p}({f}_{E,\alpha})}\in\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}_{E}}H^{2d}_{c}(Y_{G}(V_{\boldsymbol{r}}),\mathscr{V}^{\underline{k},{0}}_{L_{\mathfrak{m}}}).

We now evaluate the right hand side of (
35
) by distinguishing two cases.

(1)

Suppose that
\pi(f_{E,\alpha})_{\mathfrak{P}}
is a ramified principal series, with
f_{E,\alpha}
base change of
f_{\alpha}
, having adjoint character
\chi^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{P}}
of conductor
\mathfrak{P}^{r_{\mathfrak{p}}}
for
\mathfrak{P}|\mathfrak{p}
, with
r_{\mathfrak{p}}\geq 1
, at each
\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}
. Then, by Lemmas
7.6
and
7.4
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{[\omega_{J}(W^{*}_{\mathfrak{c}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}}g^{-\iota}),{\rm mom}^{{\underline{k}}}_{\boldsymbol{r}}\,\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}^{\chi^{-1}\theta^{2}}]}{\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{p}({f}_{E,\alpha})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\alpha^{\circ}_{p}}^{2\boldsymbol{r}}}\frac{\zeta^{p\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{F}}_{F}(2)}{a_{\underline{k}}C_{\mathcal{E},\boldsymbol{r}}}\frac{{\rm Res}_{s=0}I(\gamma_{\boldsymbol{r}}\cdot f_{E,\alpha},s)}{\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{p}({f}_{E,\alpha})}

\displaystyle=\frac{G(\chi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime})}{{\alpha^{\circ}_{p}}^{2\boldsymbol{r}}}\frac{\zeta^{p\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{F}}_{F}(2)}{a_{\underline{k}}C_{\mathcal{E},\boldsymbol{r}}}\frac{{\rm Res}_{s=0}I(f_{E,\alpha},s)}{\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{p}({f}_{E,\alpha})},

where
a_{\underline{k}}
is the constant of Lemma
4.6
,
{\alpha^{\circ}_{p}}^{2\boldsymbol{r}}=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}}{\alpha^{\circ}_{\mathfrak{p}}}^{2r_{\mathfrak{p}}}
, with
\alpha^{\circ}_{\mathfrak{p}}
the
U_{\mathfrak{p}}^{{}^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}}
-eigenvalue of
f_{\alpha}
, and
G(\chi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime})
denotes the Gauss sum of the restriction to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{\times}
of
\chi_{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}
. We have also denoted by
C_{\mathcal{E},\boldsymbol{r}}
the residue at
s=1
of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}}^{*}(h,s)
of level
\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}}
, which relates to
C_{\mathcal{E}}
by the formula

C_{\mathcal{E},\boldsymbol{r}}=\frac{|(\mathcal{O}_{F}/p^{\boldsymbol{r}+\boldsymbol{1}})^{\times}|}{\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}{\rm N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{2r_{\mathfrak{p}}+2}}C_{\mathcal{E}}.

By Proposition
7.1
, we have

\displaystyle\zeta^{p\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{F}}_{F}(2){\rm Res}_{s=0}I(f_{E,\alpha},s)

\displaystyle=C(0){\rm Res}_{s=1}L({\rm As}(g),s)

\displaystyle=C(0)L^{\mathfrak{d}_{E/F}\mathfrak{b}_{F}}(\mathrm{Ad}(f_{\alpha})\otimes\eta_{E/F},1){\rm Res}_{s=1}\zeta_{F}^{\mathfrak{d}_{E/F}p\mathfrak{c}_{F}\mathfrak{b}_{F}}(s)\prod_{\mathfrak{q}|\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}}L_{\mathfrak{q}}({\rm As}(g),1),

with
\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}:=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{q}|\mathfrak{d}_{E/F}\\
\mathfrak{q}\nmid\mathfrak{b}_{F}\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{q}
. As

C(0)=\frac{|D_{F}\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(D_{E/F})^{1/2}|^{2}|D_{F}|^{1/2}}{\pi^{d}2^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{F}}(k_{\sigma}+2)}}\prod_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{F}}L_{\sigma}(\mathrm{Ad}(f_{\alpha})\otimes\eta_{E/F},1),

the result follows.

(2)

If
\pi(f_{E,\alpha})_{\mathfrak{P}}
is either an unramified principal series or Steinberg at each
\mathfrak{P}
, then we have that
\boldsymbol{r}=\boldsymbol{0}
. The
p
-adic
L
-function evaluated at
P
is then

\displaystyle L_{p}(\mathrm{Ad}(\mathbf{f})\otimes\eta_{E/F})[P]=[K_{0}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}):\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\ast,+}K_{11}(\mathfrak{c}^{\prime})]G(\theta)\frac{[\omega_{J}(W^{*}_{\mathfrak{c}p}g^{-\iota}),{\rm mom}^{\underline{k}}_{0}\,\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}^{\chi^{-1}\theta^{2}}]}{\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{p}({f}_{E,\alpha})}.

We proceed as in the previous case, with the difference that by Lemmas
7.6
and
7.4
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{[\omega_{J}(W^{*}_{\mathfrak{c}p}g^{-\iota}),{\rm mom}^{\underline{k}}_{0}\,\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}^{\chi^{-1}\theta^{2}}]}{\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{p}({f}_{E,\alpha})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathcal{E}^{\boldsymbol{0}}_{p}(f_{\alpha})\zeta^{p\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{F}}_{F}(2)}{a_{\underline{k}}C_{\mathcal{E},\boldsymbol{0}}}\frac{{\rm Res}_{s=0}I(f_{E,\alpha},s)}{\Omega^{\varepsilon}_{p}(f_{E,\alpha})},

where
\mathcal{E}^{\boldsymbol{0}}_{p}(f_{\alpha})=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}\left(1-p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}(1+k_{\sigma})}\,\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p})({{\alpha^{\circ}_{\mathfrak{p}}}})^{-2}\right)
, with
\alpha^{\circ}_{\mathfrak{p}}
the
U_{\mathfrak{p}}^{{}^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}}
-eigenvalue of
f_{\alpha}
. If
\pi(f_{E,\alpha})_{\mathfrak{P}}
is Steinberg, we conclude as in (1). If
\pi(f_{E,\alpha})_{\mathfrak{P}}
is an unramified principal series, then
f_{\alpha}
and
f_{E,\alpha}
are
p
-stabilisations of nearly-ordinary cusp forms
f
and
f_{E}
. If
\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}
are the Satake parameters of
\pi(f)_{\mathfrak{p}}
, the value
\alpha^{\circ}_{\mathfrak{p}}
equals
p^{\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{\mathfrak{p}}}k_{\sigma}/2}\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}{\rm N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{1/2}
, hence

\mathcal{E}^{\boldsymbol{0}}_{p}(f_{\alpha})=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\in\Upsilon_{F,p}}\left(1-\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p})\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}^{-2}\right).

Moreover, we apply Lemma
7.3
to have

{\rm Res}_{s=0}I(f_{E,\alpha},s)=\left(\prod_{\mathfrak{p}\mid p}\frac{(1-\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p})\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-1})(1-\eta_{E/F}(\mathfrak{p})\frac{\beta_{\mathfrak{p}}}{\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}}\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-1})}{1+\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-1}}\right){\rm Res}_{s=0}I(f_{E},s).

Write
\zeta^{p\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{F}}_{F}(2)=\prod_{\mathfrak{p}}(1-{\rm N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-2})\zeta^{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{F}}_{F}(2)
. Then on the product
\zeta^{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{F}}_{F}(2){\rm Res}_{s=0}I(f_{E},s)
we argue as in the previous cases and get

\displaystyle\zeta^{\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}_{F}}_{F}(2){\rm Res}_{s=0}I(f_{E},s)

\displaystyle=C(0){\rm Res}_{s=1}L({\rm As}(f_{E}\otimes\theta^{-1}),s)

\displaystyle=C(0)L^{\mathfrak{d}_{E/F}\mathfrak{b}_{F}}(\mathrm{Ad}(f)\otimes\eta_{E/F},1){\rm Res}_{s=1}\zeta_{F}^{\mathfrak{d}_{E/F}\mathfrak{c}_{F}\mathfrak{b}_{F}}(s)\prod_{\mathfrak{q}|\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}}L_{\mathfrak{q}}({\rm As}(f_{E}\otimes\theta^{-1}),1).

We then conclude by noticing that

\prod_{\mathfrak{p}}\frac{1-{\rm N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-2}}{1+\mathrm{N}_{F/{\mathbb{Q}}}(\mathfrak{p})^{-1}}{\rm Res}_{s=1}\zeta_{F}^{\mathfrak{d}_{E/F}\mathfrak{c}_{F}\mathfrak{b}_{F}}(s)={\rm Res}_{s=1}\zeta_{F}^{\mathfrak{d}_{E/F}p\mathfrak{c}_{F}\mathfrak{b}_{F}}(s).

∎

Remark 7.9
.

(
\Lambda
-adic Hilbert modular forms with coefficients in cohomology)

Let
\mathcal{K}
be a large enough extension of
\mathrm{Frac}(\Lambda_{G/H})
which contains all the coefficient fields of the primitive base-change families of Proposition
6.11
. Fix a type
J
for
E/F
. For each base change family
\mathbf{f}_{E}
contributing to
\mathcal{H}^{G/H}_{11}
as in Proposition
6.11
, choose a character
\theta_{\mathbf{f}_{E}}
as in Theorem
7.8
.

Definition 7.10
.

We define an element of
e^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}H^{2d}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})\otimes_{\Lambda_{G/H}}\mathcal{K}
:

\sum_{\mathbf{f}_{E}}\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}_{E}}\mathscr{Z}_{\infty,\theta_{\mathbf{f}_{E}}}^{\flat},

where the sum runs over all the base change families from
F
of Proposition
6.11
.

This can be seen as a family version of
\Phi_{Q([Z]),\chi_{E}}
of
[
17
, Theorem 8.4]
(notation as in §8.6 of
loc. cit.
).
Similarly to Theorem 8.5 of
loc. cit.
, after identifying
e^{\mathrm{n}.\mathrm{ord}}H^{2d}_{\rm Iw}(Y_{G}(V_{\infty}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{m}})\otimes_{\Lambda_{G/H}}\mathcal{K}
with a space of
\Lambda
-adic modular forms, we can calculate the “Fourier coefficient” of this form, applying Theorem
7.8
. It would be interesting to upgrade Definition
7.10
to have a proper
\Lambda
-adic form, and not simply a sum of components in each base change family. Indeed, after applying the idempotents
\mathbf{1}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathbf{f}_{E}}
, one looses all the information on congruences (and intersection) between families.
To obtain a
\Lambda
-adic form, one would need to construct a version of
\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}
with coefficients in compactly supported cohomology (resp. intersection cohomology) and then pair it with our
\mathscr{Z}_{\infty}
(resp. with itself). The main obstacle is the generalisation of Proposition
4.17
to suitable compactifications.
